## 1. John Whitaker × [mother unknown] (P106)

Grades: 75%+ for the cluster itself; **below-75% for the identity** (the IL John may or may not be Permelia's father). A family moving Muhlenberg KY → Union Co. IL in 1814 through southern Indiana fits Permelia's "probably Indiana" ~1814/15 birth. (2026-10-02-filing-whitaker-illinois-cluster-report.md)
2. **Manley 1951 confirmation of "Mrs. Joan Whitaker"** — Manley lists her as **"JOAN"** among exactly three daughters of John Hunt × Charity Hunt (Charity, Joan, Margaret "Peggy"). Grade: DOCUMENTED (existence/parentage); her Whitaker husband remains UNKNOWN. Story already carries the 1913 history's "Mrs. Joan Whitaker" and the Muhlenberg Marriage Book 1 (1802–1836) negative (Whitaker grooms: Robert×Weir 1807, Alexander×Underwood 1813, Thomas×Davis 1826, Wiley×Davis 1827); the Manley three-daughters list is the new documentation. Attaches to the hypothesis family only, NOT proved to our John. **Aaron Whitaker** (son of James3, cousin line) served in the Washington County militia, Rev War — CLAIMED (monograph only). **James Whitaker** (b. 1790, cousin line) served in the War of 1812 — CLAIMED (monograph only). The monograph records **no military service for John4 himself**. Our John's own service: NONE-FOUND (no Rev War or War of 1812 record; primary rolls unchecked — Fold3/NARA lane parked). ## 5. Nimrod Foster × Elizabeth Brown (P122)
**Story matches the prior research; the 2026-10-02 dig STRENGTHENS the story's verdict with two new compiled sources.** The story's "sole source" language is now OUTDATED — the parent link rests on **three independent compilations**, corroborated but still derivative. **FACTS TO ADD** (all compiled/derivative claims — carry as attributed; the audit grades each 75%+ as compiled-genealogy claims; the "very likely, derivative" verdict stands):
1. **Elizabeth's mother: Jane (Doak)** — story says "Jane Brown." (Karabogias & Norman, *Descendants of William Brown*, 86 pp., archive.org — documents/descendants-of-william-brown-d1761.pdf)
2. **Nimrod's mother: Sarah (Hughes)** — story says "Sarah Foster." Her brother **Thomas Hughes** built a fort on Laurel Creek, Nicholas Co., during the latter part of the Revolution. (Flavius Foster, *Seedlings of William Foster*, 130 pp., wvancestry.com, quoting William Griffee Brown, *History of Nicholas County West Virginia*, Dietz Press 1954, pp. 318–319 — documents/seedlings-of-william-foster.pdf)
3. **Scotch-Irish ethnicity**: W.G. Brown's county history labels the Fosters "Scotch-Irish." Elizabeth's grandfather **William Brown** (d. 19 May 1761, Augusta Co) was an **Ulster Irish immigrant**: proved importation in Orange Co VA court **22 May 1742** (County Ulster, Ireland → Philadelphia → Prince William Co VA → Beverly Manor patent, 1740); served in the **Augusta County Militia, 1742**; member of **Tinkling Spring meeting house** (Presbyterian). — The story's ethnicity section currently says "English, by the names"; these attributed compiled findings supersede it.
4. **Nimrod was a consumptive (tuberculosis)** — W.G. Brown's county history: he "was a consumptive, and the records show he was released from work on the roads." Health/old-age detail; also the plausible explanation for his absence from Rev War militia rolls (military sweep: Nimrod service NONE-FOUND; lane stays nominally open on Prince William militia rolls).
5. **Migration path: m. in Prince William Co; 1795 to Monroe Co; 1800 to Nicholas Co** (both buried Nicholas Co, Bend of Gauley area). Isaac Foster × Sarah Hughes also buried at Bend of Gauley. Story's current path is "Prince William to Kanawha to Nicholas."
6. **Daughter Elizabeth m. David Miller 11 Aug 1807** (specific date).
7. **Alexander "Sandy" = Grigsby** (same person per Flavius Foster); m. Martha Baker Handley 3 Mar 1791.
8. **Docea/Docena m. James Boggs 14 Mar 1804 — "who deserted her and their children"** (family drama, from Flavius Foster); Dicena b. 1 Jul 1788, d. 2 Jan 1870.
9. **Children's marriages**: Peyton m. Nancy Johnson 11 Jan 1815; Turley m. Amy Johnson 16 Nov 1816; Presley m. Elizabeth Johnson 12 Mar 1822 and Lucretia Bailes 9 Feb 1840; Michael Miles m. Mary Scott 1804.
10. **William Brown III** (Elizabeth's brother, b. 1759 Augusta Co, d. 1823 Monroe Co): **served the Colonies in the American Revolution** (unit not given) — the family's proved service. (Brother-in-law, not the couple — carry in the military/wars section.)
11. **Isaac Foster's line (Nimrod's father)**: b. 2 Jul 1719 Northumberland VA, d. 1814; m. Sarah Hughes 1752, Prince William VA; bought land 10 Apr 1775 at South Run/Broad Run (now under **Lake Manassas**); sold 1790 in a **"twig & turf" ceremony**; moved to Monroe Co (Swopes Knob); 1797 gave the land to son Nathaniel and moved to Nicholas Co.
12. **Thomas Hughes** (uncle-in-law): Rev. War Pvt., Capt. Smith's Augusta Militia (per WeRelate — needs verification); will 22 May 1793, Kanawha Deed Book A p. 26; built fort at the mouth of Hughes Creek / Laurel Creek. Lore-adjacent, carry attributed.
13. Update the story's verdict language: parent link now corroborated by three compilations (Filae + Brown/Norman + Foster/Brown) — grade unchanged ("very likely, derivative").
**Lore (backup status):** story's lore column is empty and labeled. New lore-adjacent items: Hughes Fort / Laurel Creek frontier lore; "Docea's husband deserted her" (family drama); the "twig & turf" 1790 land-sale ceremony (custom lore). All UNPROVED, carry as attributed tales.
**Flags (UNRESOLVED — below 75%, do NOT resolve in the story):** Nimrod's death year — Flavius Foster says **d. 1820** vs Geni/story **13 Jun 1818** (needs the will/burial). "A son George" (story's §307 will quotation; 1810 Monroe census has George Foster heading a household) vs the Brown/Norman child list (no George) — George's identity open. 1794 Samuel Brown estate sale county (Prince William vs Monroe — Morton's book is a Monroe Co. history; the story places it in Prince William).
**PICTURES TO ADD:** none filed (the two compilation PDFs are documents, not pictures).

## (5) William Casto Sr. × Hannah Bonnett — folder `william-casto-sr-hannah-bonnett`

**Story state:** COMPILED reconstruction (b. 9 Jul 1769, son of Azariah per Don Norman; d. 1836, will probated 17 Dec 1836; Mount Moriah church records; children roster). Mother conflict (Margaret Patsy Casto vs. Hannah Bonnett) correctly flagged in-story.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. PARENTAGE/BIRTH CONTRADICTED PAIR — FLAG ONLY: story says b. 9 Jul 1769, son of Azariah Casto (Don Norman compiled); tree says b. 12 Jan 1760 Turkey Point Corner, Cumberland Co NJ, son of David D. Casto I × Phoebe (Gandy) (P131 verification; audit). Never reconciled. The NJ origin is tree-level compiled — no primary birth record. Flag for Rick's decision; do not change the story.
2. HANNAH'S IMPOSSIBLE DATES — FLAG: tree gives b. 20 Apr 1772 Cedar Creek, Harrison Co; Find a Grave transcription on William's profile says "married to Hannah BONNETT about 1783 in Fayette Co PA" — age 11 at marriage. One of the two is wrong (audit).
3. FULL MIGRATION PATH (story covers PA→WV; NJ origin + deed detail are the adds): Turkey Point Corner, Cumberland Co NJ (tree, 1760 — compiled) → Fayette Co PA (Mount Moriah Baptist: Hannah joined by letter 11 Dec 1791; William baptized Nov 1797; both dismissed by letter 8 Sep 1798) → Randolph Co VA (road order 27 Nov 1797; bought 250 acres $100 on 6 Oct 1805; sold to Abraham Carper $500 on 14 Aug 1814) → Cedar Lakes, Jackson Co (1816; d. 1836; will probated 17 Dec 1836) (audit).
4. BONNETT SIDE EARLY: "Samuel Bonet" on John Sleeth's 1785 Harrison Co tithables list (mouth of Lost Creek up the West Fork) — Hannah's father Samuel Bonnett Sr. in Harrison County by 1785, before her 1791 church-joining (audit; genealogytrails 1785 census).
5. SON JONATHAN'S WAR OF 1812: carried on the tree as "Jonathan Casto (War of 1812)" — SERVICE ASSERTED, UNIT UNKNOWN; the nine-children roster in the story carries no service note (2026-10-02-military-jonathan-casto-1812.md). BROWSER-NEEDED (NPS 1812 soldier database; VA militia rosters).
6. BOOK HIT: Don Norman, *Descendants of Azariah Casto* (compiled genealogy, posted 18 Dec 2014) — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION: "William Casto, a son of William Casto, was born July 9, 1769 and died in Jackson County VA (WV) in 1836. (His will was probated December 17, 1836.) He married Hannah Bonnet. Hannah died about 1810 and William married Martha 'Patsy' Parsons in 1816." Nine children listed (James, William, Lucretia, Mary Ann "Nancy", Jonathan, Benjamin, John G., Rhoda, Absolom) with record citations (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md). FLAG: "married Martha 'Patsy' Parsons in 1816" vs. the kid-story verdict that Patsy was Devil Bill's wife — which William married Patsy is itself conflicted (tree's profile lists three marriages: Patsy (Martha) Parsons 1795; Margaret; Hannah — internally inconsistent per P131 verification).
7. PERIODICAL LEAD: *Casto Connections* newsletter, Issue 8 (Apr 1999): "William Casto (1760–1835) m. abt. 1793 Hannah Bonnett" — the 1760 birth variant (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md).
8. RESEARCH LANE: WVU Archives, Minnie McWhorter genealogies (A&M 0928) include BONNETT and CASTO family genealogies — untapped (audit).
9. CASTO NAME ORIGIN: Genealogy.com Casto forum discusses French origin of the name — UNPROVED (audit).

**Story state:** matches research on all key points: father PROVED (DAR A088124; 4 Jan 1849 heirs' deed Deed Book 7 pp. 272–274); mother UNRESOLVED ("Anne Elizabeth Parsons" = Nancy Ann Flesher/Sleeth reconstruction, same person — naming conflict on locked MP); scout tradition compiled/unbacked; bear-dog legend labeled; two-Marthas warning; DAR file section. ### FACTS TO ADD
1. BURIAL PLACE: Baptist Grove Cemetery, Frozen Camp, Jackson Co WV, 1/4 mile north of Route 33 — with a 1976 DAR plaque placed there recognizing him for Revolutionary and Indian War service. 75%+ (WikiTree Parsons-56 deep-dig in audit; military sweep). The story's burial section names no cemetery.
2. THE 1819 CONVEYANCE: the 1849 suit was over property on the Left Hand Fork of Mill Creek that Charles left to sons Elias and Charles Jr. on 10 Feb 1819; plaintiffs included children and grandchildren of "Capt. William Parsons, dec'd" (Clark Blake's indenture research via audit). 75%+ (deed-based) — the story's 1849 section lacks the 1819 conveyance.
3. NANCY ANN'S 1846 PENSION APPLICATION: 25 Jul 1846, age 85, Jackson Co VA — applied for benefits AS THE WIDOW OF JOHN SLEATH, Revolutionary soldier from Augusta Co (her FIRST husband, not Charles). Primary-record lead, NOT YET PULLED (audit). Browser-needed: NARA Rev. War pensions.
4. SON "CAPTAIN BILLY" PARSONS: War of 1812 militia captain — headstone at Baptist Grove reads "Capt. 6 Regt VA Militia War of 1812, 1765–1839" (N.S.U.S.D. 1812 marker); WikiTree Parsons-56 (citing JCHS Quarterly 19:2, Apr 1987): "marched from Point Pleasant up the Great Kanawha and through to Norfolk, VA." Double-sourced (military sweep).
5. DAR SPOUSE NAMING: A088124 lists spouses (1) Elizabeth Chestnut, (2) Anna Nancy Flesher Sleeth — DAR's own naming corroborates the folder's verdict that "Anne Elizabeth Parsons" = the Flesher/Sleith reconstruction (military sweep).
6. SERVICE CORRECTION (already matched in-story): the DAR record documents only 1783 supply-tax PATRIOTIC service — no rank, no unit, no combat; the 1976 plaque's "Indian War service" is a plaque claim only (military sweep).
7. BIRTH-DATE CONFLICT — FLAG: 4 Nov 1745 (Geni field, WikiTree bio, story) vs. 11 Apr 1745 (WikiTree structured field / Geni About findagrave quote) (audit; deepdig-charles-maryland-birth).
8. SECOND-MARRIAGE DATE CONFLICT — FLAG: 30 Aug 1796 (marriage index) vs. 3 May 1797 (WikiTree bio text / Charles's About) (audit).
9. ELIZABETH CHESTNUT PARENTAGE — FLAG ONLY: the James Chestnut × Margaret Ross attachment is tree-only; the Homer Jones letter documents James's children as John, James, Margaret — NO Elizabeth (filing-parsons-brief139-background). Keep parents UNKNOWN; do not attach.
10. IMMIGRANT-STATUS CORRECTION: Maryland birthplace (Queen Anne's Co) is supported by two independent tree sources (Geni + WikiTree) — Charles is NOT an immigrant; the earlier "~1740 England" claim is CONTRADICTED. Uebele's 1995 Prussia-origin tradition = unproved tradition (deepdig-charles-maryland-birth; geni-charles-maryland).
11. TREE-CHAT QUESTION (not a story add): "Immigrant" William Parsons × Johanna Christina Zedig may be conflated with the famous William Parsons surveyor (b. 1701 England, m. Johanna Christiana Zeidig Philadelphia 1722) — no ship work until the identity is settled (colonial-parsons-crossing).

## (1) Jacob Polsley × Margaret Haymond — folder `jacob-polsley-margaret-haymond`
**Story section:** ch7 lines ~16421–16920. Story audited: MATCHES research (Weston-link gate stated first; two-version Rev. service reported without picking; Maj. William Haymond gated; Baltzli legend fenced).

### FACTS TO ADD
- Maj. William Haymond's Revolutionary service is now triple-checked: commissioned militia captain by Gov. Patrick Henry on 13 March 1776; commanded Prickett's Fort in 1777; commissioned major by Gov. Benjamin Harrison on 12 Nov 1781; the full 1777 company roll survives (officers M. Morgan Morgan, James Johnston, Zarah Ozban; ~23 privates incl. Thomas Haymond); sheriff of Monongalia County 1783; post-war JP, deputy surveyor, commissioner for unpatented land claims, principal surveyor of Harrison County 1784 — still carries the Weston-link gate, but the service detail is documented. (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
- Haymond's French & Indian War service is documented: joined the Braddock expedition in 1755 at age 15 and survived the Battle of the Monongahela; Forbes Expedition 1758 that took Fort Duquesne; Virginia Regiment 1759–1762. (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
- Analysis-grade one-liner: the Jacob-as-"major" family version likely conflates Jacob with his father-in-law Maj. William Haymond — the "Major" title belongs to the Haymond line (framed as analysis, not record). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
- Dunnington's 1880 *History and Progress of the County of Marion, West Virginia* (pp. 74–75, quoted via wvgw.net excerpt): "Sometime after 1790, Jacob Polsley bought some land on Trader Island. His wife was Margaret Haymond, daughter of William's" — the couple's land is the site of the town of Palatine; Daniel and John laid out Palatine in 1838. (Grade: USEFUL-adjacent — quote verified from the excerpt; book text itself not opened.) (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
- Named-source citation to add: Katherine Alice (Polsley) Bryan, *Genealogy of the Baltzly-Balsley-Polsley Family, with other variations of the name — also a sketch of Major William Haymond* (F. J. Heer Printing Co., Columbus, Ohio, 1939, 380 pp.) — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION; the book the story already leans on; cites Julia Polsley's testimony via the DAR record chain (DAR National No. 73637) and the Pennsylvania Archives series refs (2nd Ser. Vol. XV p. 764; 3rd Ser. Vol. XXI p. 352; 5th Ser. Vol. IV p. 462). (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)

## (1) Charles W. Walker Sr. × Margaret Elizabeth Walker (folder: charles-w-walker-sr-margaret-elizabeth-walker/)

### FACTS TO ADD (each one line)
1. **CORRECTION — Charles Walker served in the Revolution (75%+):** a plaque on the Pearisburg courthouse grounds lists "John Peters and Charles Walker" as soldiers of the Rev. War, and the genealogy.com Emer Gunter tree states "Charles Walker, John and Christian Peters, with their families, in the spring of 1782, left their valley homes, crossed the Alleghenies and located in the New River Valley. They had been soldiers in the American Army." The story's current line — "no service found for Charles in the Revolution" — is superseded. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md, filed in the john-jacob-peters-sr-frances-simms folder)
2. **June 27, 1807 ferry petition:** Charles Walker headed a list of a dozen signatures on a petition to assist John Peters in gaining rights to establish a ferry on New River adjacent to Peters' dwelling, "in order to aid public convenience" — Charles and Margaret's only surviving civic act on record. (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md, via Glennis R. Walker, *The History of the Walker Family 1734–1990*, pp. 22–23, quoted on compiled Genopro page — book text not opened)
3. **Precise settlement location:** the Charles and Margaret Walker family "settled on the east side of New River opposite the mouth of Wolf Creek, this being nearby the present-day town of Narrows, Giles County, Virginia" (then Montgomery County until 1806); "The John Peters Family settled a short distance further up the river, while Thomas, the nephew of Charles; and Christian Peters went a distance further and settled in Monroe County." (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md, same Walker family history)
4. **First Giles County grand jury, June 10, 1806:** "John Peters" and "Charles Walker" both sat on the county's first impaneled grand jury, alongside William Smith (foreman) and 13 others — the two brothers-in-law together in the county's founding record. (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md, via *1806 Giles County 1956: A Brief History*, verified from the source text, INDEX-LISTING grade)
5. **Christianos's regimental command (son):** Sergeant Christianos Walker, Capt. George W. Camp's company, 4th Regiment of Virginia Militia, Sep 1813–Mar 1814, served under Col. Beaty (Majors Waggoner and Connor), brigade commander Gen. Robert B. Taylor; the 4th was stationed "in the rear of Fort Norfolk" per the May 5, 1813 general orders. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
6. **Craney Island context (son's service, 75%+):** Christianos's garrison existed to hold Norfolk after the 22 June 1813 Battle of Craney Island — British repulsed with zero American casualties — but he MISSED the battle by two months (mustered Sep 1813); his war was the cold, disease-ridden winter garrison duty after the victory, not the battle. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)

### LORE
- No new lore. The military deep-dive adds only published-history context (Craney Island) and the plaque tradition (Pearisburg courthouse) — all labeled published/institutional, backup status given.

## (2) John Jacob Peters Sr. × Frances Simms (folder: john-jacob-peters-sr-frances-simms/)

### FACTS TO ADD (each one line)
1. **§J COMPLETE — Pension S5897 (75%+):** John's own 1832 declaration (applied 31 Jul 1832, Giles Co., age 77, pension allowed): ensign, two 3-month tours — (1) 1779/80, Capt. John Rush's company, Col. Nall's Virginia Regiment, marched Rockingham→Richmond, no general engagement, just before Gates's defeat at Camden (16 Aug 1780); (2) 1781, Capt. Ragen/Rogers, Cols. William Nall → Dark → Matthews, discharged by Gen. Stephenson (for losing baggage) just before the surrender of Cornwallis, in the marches with Lafayette, Wayne, Steuben, and Washington toward Yorktown. The story's "where he served, in what regiment, in what actions, the free sources do not say" is superseded. 6. **1789 move to Pearisburg; 1808 deeds; brick home (75%+ compiled):** John and Frances moved to Pearisburg in 1789; bought two tracts later deeded to sons in 1808 (50 acres south side of New River; 30 acres on Laurel Creek); built a brick home which stood beside the Giles County Courthouse. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-john-jacob-peters-sr.md, via WeRelate — Giles deed books unexamined)

Frances Simms 30 Jun 1809 Tazewell) is a DIFFERENT man. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-john-jacob-peters-sr.md, Johnston + WikiTree cross-match)
8. **"Capt. Jack" (75%+, published):** John, son of Christian Peters — the senior's grandson — "also served as Captain in this war [1812] and was called Capt. Jack." (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md, via Pratt's *Peters Family of Giles County*, p. 52, citing Johnston)

Peters — "among the best, truest and bravest Confederate soldiers that fought for the South" (units unopened). (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md, via Barrera citing Johnston)
10. **First Giles County grand jury, June 10, 1806 (published, verified):** "John Peters" sat on the county's first grand jury (see item 4 of couple 1). (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md, *1806 Giles County 1956: A Brief History*)
11. **1807 ferry petition (published, quoted):** Charles Walker headed a petition to assist John Peters in establishing a ferry on New River adjacent to Peters' dwelling (see item 2 of couple 1). (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md, Glennis R. Walker 1990, pp. 22–23)

## (3) David "Lonesome Dave" Williams × Mary Elizabeth McGriff — "Lonesome Dave" (ch7 ~30117–30455)
Folder: `david-williams-mary-elizabeth-mcgriff/`

### FACTS TO ADD
1. **The Oct 30, 1813 muster roll of Capt. George W. Camp's Company, 4th Regiment, Virginia Militia lists "David Williams" as a private** — transcription at genealogy-quest.com of the NARA rolls (transcriber saw the originals 29 Jun 1995), pulled 2026-10-02. The roll itself is graded [75%+]; it SUPERSEDES the story's now-outdated statement "No roster has been found that ties a War of 1812 service to the correct David Williams." Propose as an adjudication add: carry the roll as a lead, not proof — whether the name is Lonesome Dave (b. 1776, age 37 in 1813, of the right county) is UNPROVED (flagged below). (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md; 2026-10-02-audit-david-williams.md)
2. **The unit's texture** (same verified transcription): the company was formed in Giles County, marched 200+ miles to Norfolk; actual field commander Lt. Ralph Lucas (b. 1788, Giles Co., schoolteacher, later sheriff) with Capt. Camp detached as Regimental Inspector; officers Lt. Andrew Caldwell and Ensign John Peters; service mid-Sep 1813 to mid-Mar 1814; camped at Fort Norfolk / the Peach Orchard at its rear and 40 days at Lynnhaven Bay; winter 1813/14 unusually cold, British fleet in Lynnhaven Bay and Hampton Roads, garrison winter with no pitched battle; discharged at Fort Norfolk; regimental commanders Lt. Cols. James McDowell, John Koontz (dismissed by general court-martial 21 Mar 1814), Thomas Chilton. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
3. **Johnston 1906, *A History of Middle New River Settlements and Contiguous Territory* (pp. 42–44), verified from the full Internet Archive text** — the Sunday, 7 Aug 1774 Indian attack at Lybrook's and McGriff's on the New River below the mouth of Sinking Creek: three little boys — Theophilus Snidow, **Thomas McGriff**, Jacob Snidow — captured and carried off; after two or three days they planned their escape, reached Pipestem Knob (now Summers Co.), camped the night; Jacob Snidow and Thomas McGriff slipped away and crawled into a hollow log; the Indians "even stood on the log in which the boys were hidden, and in broken English cried 'Come back, get lost'"; the boys made their way to Culbertson's bottom on the New River. Also: "Philip Lybrook and a man by the name of McGriff had built their cabins in a little bottom just below the mouth of Sinking creek"; the attack killed three Lybrook children, a young woman named Scott, and two little girls of Mrs. Snidow; "Mr. McGriflf shot and mortally wounded one of the Indians." Published county history, verified from full text [75%+] — epic verified McGriff-frontier story material the chapter does not yet carry (it directly neighbors the rescue tale and clarifies it: the documented captivity story has Thomas McGriff as a boy escaping on his own in 1774). (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

### FLAGGED (not proposed)
- "David Williams" (1813 roll) = Lonesome Dave — identity UNPROVED (roll gives no age or residence); flag only. A David Williams pension/bounty file for the company's men would settle it — open lead. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md; 2026-10-02-dig-williams-expanded.md)
- The 1950 article's War of 1812 claim ("also served in the war of 1812") stays UNPROVED — the roll is a lead, not proof; flag only. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)

Thomas Cocke in the 1660s–70s — a Bisse in the family's court orbit a generation before Sarah Barker's compiled "Bisse" third marriage; the lane to prove or retire the Bisse claim. *(2026-10-02-dig-hamlin-expanded.md)*
5. (supported-tertiary, Hening's Statutes Oct 1748 ferry act via USGenWeb) The ferry act reads "From Westover, in Charles City county, to Maycox, or Coggins point; and from Maycox to Westover (7 1/2d.)" — the same 7.5-pence rate as the story's 1705 statute, still running 28 years after Elizabeth's death; the story's "ran on without her for twenty years" is confirmed and can be extended. *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
6. (lead-grade, WikiTree Taylor-958) Elizabeth d. 7 June 1720 — narrows the story's "first days of June" (Byrd diary: illness from 8 May, funeral 10 June 1720). *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
7. (supported-tertiary, Wikipedia "Maycock Plantation" 2026-10-02) Weyanoke prehistory of the Maycocks tract: Weyanoke people from ~8000 BC, seven archaeological sites (Early Archaic–Late Woodland), agricultural culture; land usurped; Weyanoke left 1644 after the Anglo-Powhatan Wars. *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
8. (supported-tertiary, Wikipedia "Maycock Plantation" 2026-10-02) Maycocks settled by Samuel Maycock ~1618/1619, among the first plantations on the James's south side; tract now in the James River National Wildlife Refuge off Flowerdew Hundred Road; Fry–Jefferson map (1752) shows "Maycox Plantation" between Jordan's Point and Powell's Creek. *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
9. (supported-tertiary, Wikipedia "Maycock Plantation" 2026-10-02) Powell's Creek mill damaged in the Civil War, later restored. *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
10. (supported-tertiary) Prince George County was formed from Charles City in 1703 — Elizabeth didn't move; the county line moved under her (explains the story's 1704 "Elizabeth Hamlin of Prince George County" rent roll entry). *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*
11. (supported-secondary, WikiTree Hamlin-93 citing the deed) The Dec 1697 Roger Drayton→John Hamlin Maycocks deed styles the buyer "Capt. John Hamlin" — a third corroborating leg for the militia captaincy. *(2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)*
12. (supported-secondary, military deep-dive) The 1680 Charles City militia company: commanded by the county lieutenant (name unrecovered), under the Governor of Virginia; no regimental structure in the modern sense; no battles or campaigns attach to a 1680 Charles City captaincy — Bacon's Rebellion was 1676, King William's War (1688–97) touched Virginia's frontier, not Charles City. *(2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md / 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)*
13. (lead-grade, WikiTree research page citing the usual authorities) The immigrant Stephen Hamlin (the captain's grandfather) is a Jamestowne Society qualifying ancestor — a possible Jamestowne application lane through him, though the couple themselves carry no lineage-society line (the story's "no lineage society claims them" stays accurate as written). *(2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)*
14. Story's grades stand, not superseded. *(2026-10-02-dig-elizabeth-taylor-hamlin.md)*

### FACTS TO ADD
1. **Conflation hazard verified:** at least two later Henrico County namesakes are NOT the Cornwall emigrant brother — (a) Thomas Cocke of Malvern Hill (c.1638–1697), son of Lt. Col. Richard Cocke of Bremo (sheriff, burgess 1677, justice); (b) Capt. Thomas Cocke (b. est. 1662, d. 1707), son of the Malvern Hill Thomas (burgess 1696–1702, sheriff 1699). ## Couple 1 — John Williams of Scituate × Ann Williams ("John Williams, of Scituate × Ann Williams — the guardians behind the Barker estate"; ch14 lines 5006–5310)
Folder: `john-williams-of-scituate-ann-williams` (P4970)

Story already carries: verdict Supported as parents of Ann (Williams) Barker Blish; Newhall's plain statement ("Ann, dau. of John and Ann Williams of Scituate"); the flag pairs (birth 1618 vs c.1615; death Feb 16, 1657 vs 1658) read as generation-mixing with the daughter's dates; the full daughter-story arc (Barker ferry £131, drowning Dec 14, 1652, Blush remarriage 1653, Ann d. Feb 16, 1657/8); the guardianship suit (young John Barker "prosecuted his uncle John Williams for misuse of his office as guardian"); young John's King Philip's War service, deputy service (1689/1706/1712/1719), justice of the peace, referee, three marriages, seven children with dates, the Samuel Williams given name; Scituate as the "men of Kent" second town; the Kentish-origins lane; Deborah × William Barden and their eight children; Ichabod and Deborah Barden orphaned 1724; Scituate town books / Plymouth Colony court records / Kentish founders' lists named as the OPEN lanes; no multiples, no military for the grandparents' generation.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. The 1634 garrison house SURVIVES — it is the Williams-Barker House, today the Barker Tavern, 21 Barker Road, Scituate MA (coords 42°12.211' N, 70°43.6' W per HMDB). The 1930 Massachusetts Bay Colony Tercentenary Commission plaque at the property reads: "A portion of the house built by John Williams in 1634 is incorporated in the present house. It was a 'garrison' or place of refuge and defense during King Philip's War." → (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-williams-garrison-house.md)
2. Plaque-year CORRECTION: Wikipedia's caption says the plaque reads "1654" — the 1930 official publication (text revised by Samuel Eliot Morison) and the HMDB transcription both read 1634. Wikipedia's "1654" is a typo; use 1634. → (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-williams-garrison-house.md)
3. Samuel Deane, *History of Scituate* (1831), family sketch "JOHN WILLIAMS" (read verbatim from the Internet Archive scan): John Williams "came, we believe, with Mr. Hatherly from London 1632, and took up a farm on the north side of Scituate Harbour, very early"; "The house which was erected by John Williams as early as 1634... was a garrison house, as we know from records that it was"; wife Ann; children John, Ann, Edward, Mary; "Ann, the oldest of the family, married John Barker of Duxbury 1632." → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
4. Deane's "oldest house in Scituate" is HEDGED TRADITION — Deane wrote "It is the oldest house in Scituate, if this be the original house" and "if tradition be true, there is one part of the original building preserved" — carry as tradition, not proved fact; the 1634 construction date is tradition + plaque, NOT verified by dendrochronology. → (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-williams-garrison-house.md)
5. Newhall (1900, opened in full text): "It was built by John Williams, the father-in-law of John 1 Barker, as early as 1634, according to Deane, and was for a time used as a garrison-house, as appears from its massive inner walls of brick pierced with loopholes." → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
6. The Barker chain of title 1694–1900 (per Newhall; family history, not deed-verified): 1694 Capt. John Williams (the son) died childless, left house + 200 acres to grand-nephew Samuel Williams Barker, then age 8; 1743 Williams Barker sold half the farm for £569 to his son Samuel; 1792 Williams Barker (last of John Barker's descendants to live in Scituate) sold house and farm for £1,000 to Benjamin Barker of Pembroke on his removal to Wiscasset, Maine; after Benjamin's death it fell to his younger son Samuel P., whose son Samuel P. Barker "still lives in the homestead with his son Otis" in 1900 — "six, perhaps seven, generations of Barkers have occupied the old house." → (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-williams-garrison-house.md) — flag as Newhall's account, deed-level confirmation not attempted
7. Son Capt. John Williams commanded the right wing of the ambuscade when King Philip fell; died 1694 childless; his 1691 will: "To my two boys, George and Thomas, whom I obtained, 'with my sword and my bow,' on condition that they take my name of Williams, lands at Showamett" — Deane's note: "these were Indians without doubt." → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md) — LORE labeled: Deane 1831, verbatim; the Native context is Deane's own gloss
8. Newhall gives Deborah Barker's marriage explicitly: "4 Deborah 2 (1) m. July, 1660, William Barden of Barnstable, afterwards of Middleboro, b. 1624X, d. 1691 (will Mar. 22, 1691-2)" — the story has Deborah × Barden without the July 1660 month; this is the Barker→Barden link in print. → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
9. CONFLICT — arrival: Deane (opened 2026-10-02) says John came "with Mr. Hatherly from London 1632," while the folder's audit note carries "John's ship (William and Thomas 1618)" from the ch13 Barker chapter. UNRECONCILED — both live in the folder; the story's "Kentish lane" should note the fork. → (2026-10-02-audit-john-williams-of-scituate.md vs book-mentions-2026-10-02.md) — CONTRADICTED/UNPROVED flag
10. Ann's name itself is uncertain on the tree — the Geni-side note enters her as "Married Ann (perhaps) Williams," i.e. even her name is uncertain; the story treats "Ann" as the tree's name without the hedge. → (2026-10-02-filing-geni-batch-d-findings.md, via research-note)
11. Military sweep verdict: NONE-FOUND — John died 1657/58, before King Philip's War; Pequot War (1636–38) is the only conflict in his adult lifetime but no muster/service record in Plymouth Colony records or free sources; wife Ann, no service — confirms the story's "no military claimed" as a clean negative. → (2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
12. Modern history is UNVERIFIED single-source and must NOT go in the book as fact: Wikipedia says families occupied the house until sold in 1910, became the Hatherly Inn in the early 20th century, the Jordan family purchased it in 1978 and began operating it as the Barker Tavern — the two cited pages were not directly viewable; keep as UNVERIFIED. → (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-williams-garrison-house.md)
13. The story's illustration is currently an imagined Scituate scene — the folder now holds a period engraving of the ACTUAL property (see PICTURES). ## Couple 4 — Moses Simmons × Sarah Simmons ("The Fortune's Thanksgiving Guest"; ch14 lines 5909–6283)
Folder: `moses-simmons-sarah-simmons` (P4974)

Story already carries: verdict proved (Fortune 1621, First Thanksgiving presence, Duxbury surveyor, 1626 Purchaser, 1633 tax list, Sarah named once in the 13 Dec 1660 land sale "with consent of my wife Sarah"); Leiden 1605 birth; Winslow's *Hypocrasie Unmasked* (one parent in the Leiden church); the Jewish/Converso presumption graded unbacked and kept as family-told lore; the Fortune's crossing (55 tons, 35 settlers, Nov 9, 1621 arrival, Dec 13, 1621 return, French seizure); the harvest-feast sources (Winslow's Dec 11, 1621 letter; Bradford); the 1623 land division (2 acres shared with Philipe de la Noye) and 1627 cattle division; the Undertakers/Purchasers; de la Noye's line (Walloon, Delano line to FDR); Duxbury (40 acres 1636–39, surveyor, Gunter's chain); Sarah's invisibility and the Chandler belief (Wing, Mayflower Descendant 70, Winter 2022, "Possible Parentage"; the Roger Chandler/Solomon Leonard namesake trap; mtDNA recruiting); the thin spot on Mary's parentage (no contemporary 17th/18th-c. source naming Mary as their child); the seven children with the Mary→John eight-year gap marked; Rebecca × John Soule and the George Soule Dutch/Y-DNA material (Jan Sol × Mayken Labis); Mary × Joseph Alden ~1660 and the Bridgewater flowering; Sarah the younger × James Nash (d. June 17, 1689 — the same day her father's will was dated); the checklist (no twins, no military, no lineage societies — "the Fortune was not the Mayflower," no ancestor-authored works, Simmons 1930 history, Simmons University via John Simmons of Boston); the Sarah-Chandler believed-not-proved posture.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. Fortune DOCUMENTED from the full L.A. Simmons text (1930), opened and read 2026-10-02 — verbatim: "Cushman, commander of the 'Fortune' makes mention of Moses Simmons, one of the Pilgrims on his ship"; "The 'Fortune' sailed for America early in July, 1621, and landed at Plymouth November 9th, 1621"; Barry's History: "Moses Simmons (formerly written Moyses Symonson) came to Plymouth in the ship 'Fortune' in 1621, and is usually recorded as one of the Pilgrims." Independent passenger-roll check: "Moses Simonson" on the Fortune's roll; Dutch member of the Leiden English Separatist church; came as a single man; 1626 Purchaser as "Moyses Symonson"; name shortened to Simmons by the 1633 tax list. Grade DOCUMENTED. → (2026-10-02-deepdig-simmons-fortune.md)
2. Bertha Glidden Bradt, *Ancestors and Descendants of Samuel E. Bradt and Bertha Glidden Bradt* (DeKalb, Illinois, Dec 1943), immigrant-ancestor section verified verbatim from the opened PDF: "Moyses Simonson came to America in the Ship Fortune from Leyden, Holland (1621)"; "Cushman (Commander of 'Fortune') mentions Moses Simonson as one of his passengers, and Winslow says, 'He was a child of one that was in communion with the Dutch Church at Leyden...'"; "Pope in his 'Scrapbook of Plymouth' says that the mother of Moses Simonson in Leyden was Patience..."; "The name Simonson was changed to Simmons in 1627"; "Moses (1) Simmons was married about 1632 to Sarah (supposed to be Sarah Chandler, daughter of Roger Chandler)"; 1627 cattle division: single man's share (unmarried in 1627); in Duxbury before 1637; 40-acre grant 1638–9. → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
3. Bradt (same opened PDF) — deed trail: Old Colony Deeds Vol. N p. 64 (Dec 30, 1674 — eldest daughter Rebeckah married John Soule) and p. 274 (Dec 4, 1678 — "Moses Simmons Senir... and his wife Sarah before John Alden, Assistant") — the 1678 deed ties the Simmons and Alden stories together in one signature. → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
4. Bradt (same opened PDF) — children list: Moses, Rebecca (m. John Soule), Mary (m. Joseph Alden 1654), Elizabeth, Aaron, Sarah, John (m. Mercy Peabody 1669). New to the story: John's wife Mercy Peabody and the 1669 marriage; Mary's marriage dated 1654 (see conflict note 5). → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
5. CONFLICT — Mary × Joseph Alden marriage year: Bradt gives 1654; the story says "about 1660." UNRECONCILED — flag the fork. → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md) — UNPROVED flag on both dates pending
6. Death/will: Bradt says Moses died at Duxbury in 1691, his will probated September 15, 1691 — the story gives "will of June 17, 1689, inventory Sept 10, 1691" and "accepted reconstruction gives his own death as about 1690"; the Sept 15, 1691 probate date is new. → (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
7. Fortune voyage details (dig note): 55 tons, Master Thomas Barton; departed London fall 1621 (Simmons says early July 1621); arrived Cape Cod 9 Nov 1621; Plymouth late November; 35 settlers mostly young unmarried men; Mass Moments says "sailed into Plymouth harbor on November 10th" — one-day variance with the Nov 9 Cape Cod date, immaterial; returned 13 Dec 1621 with clapboard + 2 hogsheads of beaver/otter skins; seized by a French warship off the French coast; reached London Feb 1622 without cargo; Weston's investment lost. → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md)
8. Moses's parentage fork — BELOW 75%, stays out of narrative: OLDER TRADITION (Geni's attachment): son of William Symonson (1570–1676) × Ann Simmons; Pope's "Scrapbook of Plymouth" gives mother "Patience" in Leiden and brothers Aaron, John, Job and a sister; Arber's "Pilgrim Fathers" p.155: "the son of William Symonson who lived near the church at Leyden" — the house of Jan Robirizoon next to Willem Simonszoon's. MODERN SCHOLARSHIP (story's posture): Anderson (*Great Migration Begins* Vol. 3 p. 1681) gives parents unknown; Jeremy Bangs analyzed extant Leiden records and found no identification, but argues patronymics point to a father named Simon. Neither clears 75% — the fork stays flagged, not resolved; the folder's 2026-10-01 note overstated the Geni attachment as fact. → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md; 2026-10-02-audit-moses-simmons-sarah-simmons.md)
9. Sarah's Chandler theory UPGRADED TO REJECTED: Geni's profile EXPLICITLY REJECTS it ("not Roger Chandler, Isabella Chandler born Chilton") — the story grades it "unproved"; the folder audit says the story could be strengthened to "rejected." Note the trap the story already carries: Roger Chandler also had a daughter Sarah who married Solomon Leonard — the namesake that misled compilers. The opened Bradt text says "SUPPOSED to be Sarah Chandler, daughter of ROGER Chandler" (the Edmund-Chandler version the story cites from the DNA project is yet another variant — flag the Chandler-father fork: Edmund vs Roger). → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md; book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
10. Military sweep: NONE-FOUND — no soldier, muster, or impressment record for Moses in any free source; he was 71 when King Philip's War began — past pressing age. The story's "no military" already carries this; the sweep confirms the clean negative. → (2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
11. CAUTION (not an add — a guardrail): Pane-Joyce Genealogy, citing Watkins, *Soldiers in the Expedition to Canada in 1690* (Boston, 1898) p. 80, lists among Plymouth County men who died on the 1690 Phips Expedition to Canada: "Moses Simmons of Scituate" — but that is MOSES SIMMONS b. ca. 1666 Scituate, d. 8 Feb 1697, a different man two generations later. Do NOT attach the 1690 expedition death to the Fortune man. → (2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
12. Jewish/Converso lore — sources named: Arthur Hertzberg's *The Jews in America* (1997, p. 22); Wikipedia; "believed by many descendants" — graded unbacked, kept as told-lore; the story already carries this posture. → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md)
13. Generation numbering OPEN: book says 13th great-grandparents; Geni says 12th; Mary Simmons = 12th great-aunt (Geni) vs book's 12th great-grandmother — BELOW 75%, browser walk pending; do not renumber on Geni alone. → (2026-10-01-findings.md, via research-note)
14. Sarah's own crossing: UNKNOWN — may have come on a later ship or with family; unproved. Named in exactly one record (the 1660 land sale). → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md)
15. Rebecca × John Soule line: the best-documented collateral — the Soule Kindred research may hold Simmons-adjacent records (Duxbury land, wills); OPEN LEAD. Moses's Duxbury surveyor work: town records may name lots abutting kin — same-surname cluster work. Moses's will/probate not yet in folder — OPEN. → (2026-10-02-dig-fortune-parentage-siblings.md)

### FACTS TO ADD

## (1) James Thomas Brewster × Mary Catherine Brown Patton ("The Ferryman of Clear Fork")

### Facts to add (one line each; source note filename in parentheses)

1. CORRECTION: the 1996 resolution is **House Joint Resolution 5003** (not "HJR 500" as the story prints) — 99th GA, by Rep. Windle, Bicentennial extraordinary session; designated the SR 52 segment from Rugby to the former Brewster Bridge as the "James 'Thomas' Brewster Memorial Highway"; TDOT directed to erect signs including one "immediately adjacent to the Morgan County side of the former Brewster Bridge"; certified copy to the Transportation Commissioner and the Morgan County Commission. (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-brewstertown.md)
2. The OLD 1930 Brewster Bridge was built to replace the old Brewster Ferry (VERIFIED, 3 sources: NPS 2/13/2014 news release; HistoricBridges.org; Donna Rowland Gough) — bypassed 1999, abandoned and closed; the new 1999 bridge is the "R.M. 'Bob' Brooks Memorial Bridge"; the name "Brewster Bridge" persists in federal/NPS use (river-access page, paddling guide, Superintendent's Compendium, current maps). The two Fentress County citizens it was renamed for before 1996: UNRESOLVED — never guess names. (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-highway-bridge.md)
3. James Thomas's 1941 obituary (Morgan County News, transcribed at TNGenWeb, VERIFIED verbatim): "BREWSTER, THOMAS (UNCLE), over 80 years of age died near Rugby. He operated the ferry at Brewster Ford on the Jamestown Road. Leaves eight children and a host of grandchildren. Burial at family burying ground. Funeral on 10/28/1941." (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-brewsters-store-dig.md)
4. The Wright rescue original is VERIFIED verbatim from the 1896 *Autobiography of Rev. A. B. Wright* (pp. 120–121; Internet Archive item `autobiographyofr00wrig`): the rescue object was the HORSE, not Wright ("came very near having my horse drowned... had not Thomas Brewster... brought him out"); Wright fell on his knees praying; he reached the Mount Vernon quarterly meeting in due time; HJR 5003's "saving his life" is a legislative embellishment CONTRADICTED by the original; Gough's p.199 paraphrase confirmed accurate. (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-wright-rescue-verbatim.md)
5. James Thomas's gravestone reads "J. THOMAS BREWSTER / 1855 / OCT. 26, 1941 / DEATH IS ANOTHER LIFE." (stone says 1855 vs death cert 16 Dec 1854; Find a Grave memorial 34506529). (pictures/james-thomas-brewster-marker-2009.jpg.caption.txt)
6. Cemetery resolution: Carpenter Cemetery = Old Nydeck Cemetery (near Robbins, Morgan Co; Find a Grave ID 16439; GNIS 1306844) — the 1941 "family burying ground" (death cert "Nydeck") resolves to this ground (PROBABLE, strong); four of James Thomas's siblings have stones there (Sheron 1851–1865, Fairbea 1855–1866, Phoebe 1857–1858, George 1865–1875; memorials 34506651 / 4506515 / 34506639 / 34506520). Brewster Cemetery = Brewstertown Cemetery (same ground, FG 9679, GNIS 1278305); the family used Carpenter/Nydeck in 1941 and Brewstertown Cemetery in later generations. (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-cemeteries.md)
7. Brewstertown is a GNIS populated place (ID 1309631; 36.3448, -84.7249; on the Rugby 7.5-min quad) and had a post office 1929–1937 (PROBABLE, two sources). (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-brewstertown.md)
8. Mary's 1907 Eastern Cherokee application was witnessed by John "Preacher Johnny" Brewster. (2026-10-02-filing-myheritage-bios-paste-ready.md)
9. The two-Lucys verdict: the 1850 census's Lucy Brown, 19 (Bledsoe Co, dwelling 667) is Mary's YOUNGER SISTER/aunt, not her mother; Mary's mother Lucy (b. ~1815–1825, Sequatchie Valley) died 26 Jan 1847 two weeks postpartum; 3-year-old Mary is absent from David W. Brown's 1850 household — consistent with placement on her father's side (fits Cleo's testimony); Wilma Gibson's "Lucy b. ca.1831 d. 1852" conflates the two women. (2026-10-02-filing-lane-h-lucy-selby-evidence-2026-09-30.md)
10. Son Jesse W. Brewster: WWI veteran, Rainbow Division (42nd Infantry), discharged 1919 (VERIFIED, Morgan County News 1919 transcription); a Reverend by 1920, married Ellen "Happy" Crisp ~1920 (VERIFIED, 1920 marriages transcription); 10 children (Onvie Ray, Vernon, Juanita Bertram, Nina Myers, Daisy Hunter, Vida Wilson, Wayne, Jessie Jr., Jessica Burnett — VERIFIED via Violet Brewster Preston's 2016 obituary). (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-brewsters-store-dig.md)
11. LORE/UNBACKED — Mary Catherine's saying: "No one owns the earth, we are to take care of it, live in peace." (2026-10-02-filing-2026-10-01-brewstertown-lore.md)
12. LORE/UNBACKED — Preacher Johnny was "the first Brewster elder to live in Brewstertown"; Johnny × Rebecca ran a small Civil War-era ferry — with timeline tension (James was a child 1861–65, so the Civil-War ferry was Johnny's or the lore compresses generations). (2026-10-02-filing-2026-10-01-brewstertown-lore.md; 2026-10-02-historic-buildings-ferry-clear-fork.md)
13. LORE/UNBACKED — reunion origin: Jesse W. Brewster Sr. × Ellen's kids started the reunion for their June 10th wedding anniversary; Bill Hunter took signatures the first big year and ~300 showed up. (2026-10-02-filing-2026-10-01-brewstertown-lore.md; 2026-10-02-filing-messenger-threads-2026-10-01.md)
14. LORE/UNPROVED (single source) — Brewster's Store burned 19 May 1956 (a Saturday); store operator unknown, Jesse W. is the leading candidate by photo association only. (2026-10-02-historic-buildings-brewsters-store-dig.md)
15. The 1900 census boys "Chas A" (12, b. ~1888) and "Walter J" (9, b. ~1891) are attributed to THIS household (previously misattributed to George Bailey × Letitia Warden) — HIGH confidence; they match Charley Albert and James Walter in Mary's sworn ten. ## (3) Johann Lorch × Theresa Novak ("The Carpenter and the Laundress")

### Facts to add

1. **Theresa + Margit crossing now VERIFIED** (2026-10-02 correction — supersedes the story's "remains unknown" and "The Theresa-and-Margaret manifest has never surfaced"): S.S. Main, Norddeutscher Lloyd, departed Bremen 9 Aug 1907, arrived Baltimore 24 Aug 1907 — manifest List 61, lines 4–5: "Lorch, Therez," 32, married; "Lorch, Margit," 14, single, traveling together. Triple-checked: manifest + independent Soundex-card index (Baltimore 24 Aug 1907) + audit corroboration. CAVEAT (carry, don't hide): manifest says "Therez" 32 (= b. ~1875) vs Theresa's documented 1 May 1863 (= 44); identification rests on the uncommon surname + "Margit" 14 matching Margaret's documented ~1893 birth + married status + Soundex. (2026-10-02-correction-theresa-main-verified.md; 2026-10-02-ship-picture-main-theresa.md)
2. S.S. Brandenburg details (Johann's ship): built 1901 at Vegesack by Bremer Vulkan; 7,532 GRT; Köln class — seven sister ships (Köln, Frankfurt, Hannover, Cassel, Breslau, Chemnitz, Brandenburg) built expressly for the Bremen–Baltimore/Galveston emigrant service (~1,600 steerage berths, ~50 cabin; triple-expansion engines, 13 knots); seized as WWI reparation, briefly in the U.S. Navy 9–20 May 1919 at Southend-on-Sea, then British Alfred Holt Steamship Co. (Blue Funnel) through 1923; renamed Hecuba 1923; scrapped in Italy, Dec 1924. (2026-10-02-lane3-johann-theresa.md; documents/johann-lorch-brandenburg-packet.md)
3. "Janos Larch" manifest line now INCONCLUSIVE (was "unbacked lead"): FamilySearch Baltimore T844 film 46 — "JANOS LARCH," 40, b. 1866, ship Brandenburg, April 1906 (ARK 1:1:2CS7-P7F); the manifest image could not be viewed (FamilySearch sign-in required); the ~4-year age discrepancy is unresolved; OCR of List 1 garbled, suggests last residence "Pap," Hungary — uncertain. Johann's ship VERIFIED rests on his sworn declaration alone. (2026-10-02-deepdig-lorch-janos-larch-manifest.md; 2026-10-02-familysearch-lorch-inconclusive.md)
4. Margaret's immigration-year wobble now anchors on the 1907 Main manifest (1920 census said 1910, 1930 said 1913 — conflicted). (2026-10-02-correction-theresa-main-verified.md)
5. Context: 149,932 emigrants left Hungary in 1906 — the third-largest year of the entire 1899–1913 wave, one year before the 1907 peak; Theresa crossed at the flood tide of Hungarian emigration. (2026-10-02-lane3-johann-theresa.md)
6. Context: NDL's Baltimore agency in this era was A. Schumacher & Co., 7 South Gay Street (per a GG Archives NDL passenger-list header, 1910) — the firm Johann would have met ashore; Baltimore was America's third emigrant port after New York and Philadelphia. (2026-10-02-lane3-johann-theresa.md)
7. Book/history sweep: NEGATIVE for this couple (Internet Archive + full text of the 1881 History of Hamilton County + Nelson's 1894 History of Cincinnati; Google Books API returned 429 — hard stop, so the Google Books sweep is incomplete). (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

### Pictures to add

- `pictures/ss-brandenburg-1901.jpg` — period NDL postcard of the S.S. Brandenburg (VERIFIED grade; public domain by age, Norway Heritage ship-images gallery). Caption must carry the "Janos Larch" age-40 caveat and must NEVER attach Theresa/Margaret to this ship. ADDABLE. (pictures/ss-brandenburg-1901.jpg.caption; 2026-10-02-ship-picture-brandenburg.md)
- `pictures/ss-main-ndl-postcard.jpg` — period NDL postcard of the S.S. Main (VERIFIED by the 1907 manifest; source mediastorehouse.de — verify rights before publication). Carry the manifest-age caveat (Therez 32 vs documented 44). ADDABLE with rights caveat. (pictures/ss-main-ndl-postcard.jpg.caption; 2026-10-02-ship-picture-main-theresa.md)

### Still unknown

## (3) George W. Shamblin × Sydna Ann Miller — folder `george-w-shamblin-sydna-ann-miller` — story "The Farm at Grass Lick"

Story status: current; audit grades story-vs-research VERIFIED and the military refutation VERIFIED. Gaps below are genuinely new since the story text.

### FACTS TO ADD
- Reuben Perry Shinn's 1923 published biography (History of West Virginia, Old and New, vol. III, pp. 422–423; verified 1923 published account, details not independently verified) — the single richest new material: Reuben (b. 20 Jul 1860, Mason Co) married Artemisia Shamblen at Grass Lick in 1882; he individually owned 2,300 acres of valuable land on Grass Lick and Poca River bottoms, kept 250–300 cattle, belonged to a syndicate owning another 2,000 acres; president of the First National Bank of Ripley; TWICE sheriff of Jackson County; member of the state Senate; Republican leader; supported all WWI drives; Odd Fellow; moved to Ripley Nov 1912 but stayed in touch with his farming operations; children Cora (Ripley high school teacher), Belle, James B. (farming), Walter Warren (engineering); Artemisia herself was "actively interested" in the farming operations. The story mentions Cora and Walter but omits Reuben's public career entirely. (lore-shamblen-miller.md §1)
- The doubled farm motif: Artemisia's father-in-law George W. Shinn (1834–1897) also owned a "fine farm at Grass Lick near Fairplain"; pioneer Republican, 18 years on the Jackson County Court, represented Jackson Co in the House of Delegates when the capital was at Wheeling — two large-farm families adjoining at Grass Lick. (lore-shamblen-miller.md §1)
- Reuben P. Shinn's supposed 1911 death (Casto Connections compilation, tree material) is CONTRADICTED by the 1923 biography, which describes him in the present tense as bank president, state senator, and active farmer — do not repeat 1911 without a death record; trees need correction. (lore-shamblen-miller.md §2)
- Stone-level name-spelling texture (WVGenWeb Shamblin Cemetery transcription): Sydna's stone reads "Shamblin, Sidney (Sudna Miller)" — two variants on one stone; Josephine's stone uses the "Chamblin" spelling ("d/o W. and Sudna A. (Miller) Chamblin"); Sydna's stone confirms the Nov 4, 1858 marriage AND George's parents ("son of Jesse and Margaret (Parsons) Shamblin") on a primary source. (lore-shamblen-miller.md §3)
- Date refinements from stones: Frederick E. d. 18 Sep 1883, age 2-3-1 (implies birth ~17 Jul 1881); Thomas E. Jan 22, 1875 – Sept 6, 1931; Cena E. (Moore), wife of M. Luther Shamblin, d. 24 Apr 1890, age 19-6-4 (confirms the 21-day marriage arithmetic: m. 3 Apr, d. 24 Apr). (lore-shamblen-miller.md §3)
- Garnes Knob place-name lore — VERIFIED (Place Names of Jackson County, JCHS): highest point in Jackson County (1225 ft), named for Joseph Garnes; relevance: Sydna's sister Amanda C. Miller married D. Frank Garnes (1861–1927). Joseph/D. Frank kinship UNBACKED — use as verified landscape texture only. (lore-shamblen-miller.md §5)
- Military refutation (now full): the book's "11th West Virginia Cavalry" is institutionally impossible — West Virginia fielded Union cavalry only through the 7th; the 11th WV was an INFANTRY regiment (Dyer's Compendium) — REFUTED as written; no Shamblin variant found in 11th WV Infantry material; George's actual service (if any) remains UNPROVED; the Clay County 126th Militia namesake is unlinked. Story's in-story refutation stands. (2026-10-02-dig-military-service-refuted.md)
- HMdb 2026-10-02 verification: the 2014 Ripley courthouse marker inscription confirmed verbatim — "G.W. Shamblen captured Morgan" (John Morgan, Pfost–Greene murders, 3 Nov 1897); George would have been ~61. Marker text VERIFIED as a match to the story's lead; G.W. = our George still UNPROVED (~40%, below bar); the Nov–Dec 1897 Jackson Herald accounts would likely name the captor's full name or age — browser-needed. (2026-10-02-dig-george-shamblin.md)
- Emma F. (second wife): the only trace is the 1885 deed reference "George Shamblen and Emma F. his wife" — new document detail; maiden name/marriage record still unknown. (lore-shamblen-miller.md, Circle covered)

## (1) Walter Weston Polsley × Nancy Caroline Ridenour — folder `walter-weston-polsley-nancy-caroline-ridenour`
**Story:** "The Obituary's Sister" + dash-list rundown. The 2026-10-02 audit verified story-vs-research MATCHES — every correction from the research report is in the story. The items below are the NEW dig finds (audit + military sweep + lore, all 2026-10-02) not yet in the narrative.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. Lt. Col. John J. Polsley (b. 9 May 1831) — Walter's paternal uncle — SERVED: 8th West Virginia Volunteer Infantry → 7th West Virginia Cavalry; his letters/papers 1862–1879 are at WVU's West Virginia & Regional History Center (collection A&M 1601), covering central-WV operations, the statehood movement, guerrilla warfare, camp life, and his confinement in Libby Prison; he signed the 1863 Jones–Imboden raid report published by the WV State Archives; rank corroborated by the Official Army Register and an 1866 sudden-death obituary. — *2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md; lore-walter-polsley-nancy-ridenour.md (John J. Polsley Papers). New: unit histories verified twice over — 8th WV Infantry (organized Buffalo WV Nov 1861; Cross Keys, Second Bull Run, White Sulphur Springs, Droop Mountain; converted to mounted infantry May 1863; reorganized as 7th WV Cavalry 26 Jan 1864) and 7th WV Cavalry (Col. John H. Oley; Crook's Raid May 1864; Hunter's Raid to Lynchburg Jun 1864; Kanawha Valley duty; mustered out 1 Aug 1865; 5 officers + 28 men killed/mortally wounded, 2 officers + 201 men dead of disease). Walter was born Dec 1862 while his uncle was fighting — chapter context from a first-degree relative's own letters.*
2. Uncle John J. Polsley's 1866 sudden-death obituary (Wood County period transcription): died suddenly in Dec 1866 after appearing in normal health Saturday; former regiment members adopted resolutions of respect; court adjourned; his widow dangerously ill. — *lore-walter-polsley-nancy-ridenour.md (VERIFIED as period obituary).*

Jacob Polsley (b. 10 May 1763), Walter's great-grandfather — CLAIMED: enlisted Pennsylvania Militia 1 Aug 1780 at 17, served seven months, depreciation pay in York County militia (Sarrett's "Polsley Family Story," 1989, quoting Bryan 1939 p.140); DAR lists multiple Polsley-line descendants — DAR eligibility flagged on this line. — *2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md.*

Casto"; she of Mason Co. — the family papers' "February 25, 1877" is corrected to August 25 (the 1921 book shares the day "25" but the wrong month); the story's "courthouse paper has not been found" is now false. ## (1) Weston Polsley × Harriet Sophia Rine — "The Anchor on the Ohio" / "The Widow of Letart"

### FACTS TO ADD
- Daniel Haymond Polsley's Mason County death register: cause of death "Heart Disease," NOT consumption; parents listed as "Joe & Mary Polsley" (informant error — father was Jacob, mother Margaret Haymond). (2026-10-02-dig-weston-polsley.md)
- Daniel's burial-location conflict is UNRESOLVED: Lone Oak Cemetery (congressional bio, Wikipedia, Clio) vs. Mount Pleasant Cemetery (RootsWeb) — the story asserts Mount Pleasant without noting the conflict. (2026-10-02-dig-weston-polsley.md)
- Daniel's 16 Aug 1861 Wheeling statement, now VERIFIED VERBATIM via Virgil Lewis's *How West Virginia Was Made* (1909), p. 230: "If they proceeded now to direct a division of the State before a free expression of the people could be had, they would do a more despotic act than any ever done by the Richmond [Secession] Convention itself." (firewall-conditional — usable only behind the unproved Weston→Daniel parentage link). (lore-weston-polsley-harriet-rine.md; 2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-weston-polsley-harriet-rine.md)

(lore-weston-polsley-harriet-rine.md)

- The FULL Nelson Ogden hiding tale (story names only the title): Nelson, a Southern sympathizer, turned his horse loose in a laurel thicket and hid at his Uncle Nathan Ogden's house; Yankee sympathizers accepted Nathan's denial while Nelson lay in the adjoining room with Nathan's second son — attributed to Nelson himself, published in Nettie Bartlett Cooper's *History of Prospect Valley* (1940). SUPPORTED as attributed published recollection; also establishes Nathan Ogden as Nelson's uncle. (lore-weston-polsley-harriet-rine.md)

Oliver Brown (1753–1846) fought in the Revolution on the Massachusetts line (secondary). (lore-weston-polsley-harriet-rine.md)

## 2. Roland Hurt × Elizabeth Lawson — `roland-hurt-elizabeth-lawson`

**Story status:** The new-book story is thorough and honest (parentage refuted, deed drama, Dicy, two-Ira correction, Rev. William Lawson thread, Delila restoration, Riffe, negative book checks). ONE major folder find is missing from it — the document the story says was never recovered.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. **Nannie Hurt's WV death record (d. 11 Jul 1929, Raleigh County) names her father as "Roland Hurt."** First document ever naming Nannie's parents; elevates the Nannie-as-daughter link to ~75%, meeting Rick's 75% book threshold. The story currently says this certificate "has not yet been recovered" — it has been (verified live 2026-10-02 on the WV Division of Culture & History database; the record image 503'd, the transcription is the evidence of record). Mother's field blank. (Source: `2026-10-02-deep-dig-round1-report.md`, summarized in `2026-10-02-deed-proof-nannie-link.md`)
2. **Moses Hurt's wife was Mary Parmer (m. 1790, Henry Co. VA)** — per a 2001 genealogy.com post by James Lawson, a descendant through daughter Nancy Hurt. This is descendant testimony (tree-level), but it corroborates the filed FamilySearch verdict that "Mary Branham Conner" is a pure tree invention; the story currently reports the Conner name as contradicted-by-absence, so Parmer slots in as the named alternative. Note: the "Mary Parmer" identification comes from the Hurt deep-dig lane, not a primary record. (Source: `2026-10-02-deep-dig-round1-report.md` §4)
3. **The deed-era Moses's daughters identified:** Nancy Hurt (m. James Lawson) and Mildred "Milly" Hurt (m. Obediah Burnett Jr.) — named in the POA signer-read as Moses's daughters; consistent with Mary Parmer as their mother. (Source: `2026-10-02-deep-dig-round1-report.md`)
4. **POA re-grade (Rick's 2026-10-02 question answered):** the 1830 power of attorney does NOT refute the Moses→Rowland son link at 90–95% — a son could serve as attorney-in-fact, and Rowland was the heirs' brother-in-law by 1830 anyway. Honest grade: POA alone ~60–65%; cumulative case against sonship ~75–80%. The story already phrases this carefully ("does not say he was Moses Hurt's son," "weighs against"), so this is nuance-confirmation, not a correction — but Rick's explicit question is now answered on the record and the "refuted" grade is withdrawn to "unproved, leaning against." (Source: `2026-10-02-poa-regrade-ricks-question.md`; THREAD-6 synthesis in `2026-10-02-thread6-final-synthesis.md`)
5. **Two candidate fathers excluded (BOOK-READY, triple-checked):** William Thomas Hurt excluded (complete 1793–1811 child list, no Rowland) and James Hurt excluded (age 12 in 1801) — trims the Moses's-nephew hypotheses. (Sources: `2026-10-02-nephew-william-thomas-hurt-excluded.md`, `2026-10-02-nephew-james-hurt-excluded.md`)
6. **"Joseph Hurt of Wayne Co. d. 1813" is contradicted by primary records:** Wayne Co. KY court orders place him alive in 1824 and 1829; the 1830 POA brackets his death to Dec 1829–Nov 1830. Compiled sibling pages carrying the 1813 date should be treated as uncited. (Source: `2026-10-02-joseph-wayne-1813-death-contradicted.md`, `2026-10-02-wayne-co-hurt-cluster-court-orders.md`)

### NOT proposed (below bar / not story material)

- Wayne Co. KY Hurt-cluster abstracts (no Rowland-age Hurt 1824–1829) — inference, unproved, not book-ready.
- The Ira Hurt × Nannie marriage hypothesis (Nannie's 1880 son Ira J. → Ira Hurt, son of John) — LOW confidence, no marriage record; never propose.
- Nannie's 1929 death-record IMAGE (film 1953473, frame 01590) still ungrabbed (HTTP 503 twice); transcription is the evidence of record.

## 1. James Gray Brewster × Phoebe Julia Cox (P57)

Phillips was paid twenty dollars per annum 'for providing for James Brewster, a pauper'" — and the claim-by-claim appendix ("James died Oct. 1887, a pauper; H. L. Phillips paid $20 for burial — half-supported") need rewriting on Rick's approval. Still open: James's actual burial circumstances (d. Oct 1887). The story uses "James Gray Brewster" throughout but never establishes the middle name's provenance. Origin of "Gray" unknown — open hunt. (2026-10-01-wife-settled.md)

Brewster, b. 1835 TN. Name variants (one woman): Phebe (1860), "Julia" (1870), "Feba" (1880) — she went by both names. (2026-10-01-wife-settled.md)
4. **NEW CHILD LEAD (75%+, single Indiana record):** William E. Brewster, b. 1835 TN — named in an Indiana marriage record as son of "Jas Brewster" + "Phoeba Cox"; not in the story's roster discussion. (2026-10-01-wife-settled.md)

L. Myers ($5–$25 notes); land on waters of Rock Creek (branch of the Big South Fork), incl. 50 acres "being the land on which James Bruster now lives on which **Brusters Mill** also stands being the same James Bruster bought from Bellfield Wood." ## 3. Hymelus Patton × Nancy (#1 and #2) (P59)
**Story matches research; the old single-Nancy folder is superseded by the live two-Nancys reconstruction.** Folders: `hymelus-patton-nancy-woods/` (old telling) + `the-two-nancys-hymelus-patton/` (live).
**FACTS TO ADD:**
1. **First-marriage record FOUND (verified): "Homeleus Patton & Nancy Woods, 26 Oct 1824."** The story gives no first-marriage date (the 1850 household is its first clear record). This is the date the old "Hymelus × Nancy Woods" telling always lacked. **Top story-add candidate for the next edit.** (2026-10-01-patton-brown-dig.md, via both 2026-10-02-readthrough-reconstruction notes)
2. **2nd Tennessee Volunteer Infantry regiment context (compiled — story-add candidates):** Col. James P. T. Carter (regiment); Lt. Col. Daniel C. Trewhitt (briefly, Jan 1862); Lt. Col. James M. Melton (Stones River). At Cumberland Gap 1862: 7th Division, Army of the Ohio (Brig. Gen. George W. Morgan); 24th Brigade (Brig. Gen. Samuel P. Carter). Engagements: Mill Springs, Cumberland Gap, Stones River (supply train guard), Morgan's Raid, Knoxville Campaign, Second Cumberland Gap, Blue Springs, Campbell's Station, Bean's Station, Mossy Creek, Dandridge, Fair Garden; mustered out 3 Aug 1865. Total losses 640: 3 officers + 24 enlisted killed/mortally wounded; **4 officers + 609 enlisted died of disease/accident** (Fox, *Regimental Losses*, 1889) — Hymelus's disease-death fits the regiment's profile (disease killed 25× combat). Regimental history: Nikazy, *Forgotten Soldiers: History of the 2nd Tennessee Volunteer Infantry Regiment (USA), 1861–1865* (Heritage Books, 1996). (2026-10-02-audit-the-two-nancys-hymelus-patton.md; 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
3. **Beatty Project context (compiled family history):** Hymelus (56 at enlistment) and eldest son Billy enlisted September 1861; moved to Mill Springs, KY shortly after — for the women's safety after a threat on the life of Jesse's mother (oral history from Billy's son Jesse Patton, b. 1851, d. 1947); "only a few days" at Mill Springs when he heard the battle guns (19 Jan 1862). Billy's death year stays unresolved (1919 per Beatty vs 1915 per TNGenWeb Delk page — story already says so). (2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md, via beattyproject.org/fentress/f403.htm)
4. **LEAD (below-75% — stays OUT of the narrative):** Nancy #2 possibly found — 1880 Scott County TN, 9th Civil District, ED 130, Sheet 1 (June 1, 1880): **"Paten, Nancy A.," F, 50, widowed head of household**, Keeping House, b. TN/TN/TN; with **Sarah D., 19, daughter**, and two grandchildren (Mary E., 3; James L., 5/12). Match logic: Nancy #2 was ~32 in July 1860 (b. ~1828) vs Nancy A. 50 in June 1880 (b. ~1830) — 2-yr variance, normal; Sarah F. 2 mo. in 1860 (b. ~Apr 1860) vs Sarah D. 19 in 1880 (b. ~1861) — within 1 yr; both TN-born; widowed; only Nancy P350 head-of-household of plausible age in TN 1880. Cautions (identity NOT proven): surname "Paten" vs "Patton" (enumerator spelling, a second marriage, or a different woman); Sarah's middle initial D (1880) vs F (1860); no pension link on the census. **If the identification holds, it extends her confirmed life to June 1880** (past the Feb 1874 arrears petition). Decider: Nancy's complete NARA pension file (RG 15, T288 "Patton, Hemelion") — paid order, **parked for Rick's decision**. Image: `1880-scott-ed130-sheet1-nancy-a-paten.jpg` (NARA T9 reel 1277). (2026-10-02-readthrough-reconstruction.md)

## 4. David W. Brown × Polly Brown (P60) — "Pompey's Kin"
**Story found ("Pompey's Kin", ch6 lines ~1149–1300). TWO VERIFIED CORRECTIONS + adds.** Folders: `david-w-brown-mary-polly-brown/` (kin folder) + `pompeys-kin-david-w-brown-polly-mary-brown/` (story folder).
**Note:** the kin folder's research-note PROFILES.md (2026-10-02) says "No story for this couple" — **STALE**; the story exists in Ch6.
**FACTS TO ADD:**
1. **CORRECTION (verified) — the Lucy contradiction is RESOLVED: two different Lucys.** The story frames it as an unresolvable standoff — "Mary Catherine swore under oath that her mother Lucy died on the twenty-sixth of January, 1847. Yet the 1850 census lists a Lucy Brown, nineteen years old… the contradiction stands in the record, and it stands here." The 2026-10-01 patton-brown dig **resolved** it: **Mary's mother died 26 Jan 1847 exactly as the 1907 application says; the 19-year-old Lucy in the 1850 census is a different woman.** Reconstruction: Mary's mother = an OLDER daughter of David W. × Polly/Mary Brown (b. ~1815–1825 in the Sequatchie Valley, married William Patton, died 26 Jan 1847 two weeks postpartum); Lucy-19 (b. ~1831) = her younger sister, alive in 1850, absent by 1860 (married/dead/moved — fate open). Corroboration: **3-year-old Mary Catherine is ABSENT from David's 1850 household** — expected if her mother was dead and she was placed with her father's people (fits Cleo's testimony: Mary knew her father; both sides helped raise her). Also: **Wilma Gibson's "Lucy Brown b. ca. 1831 d. 1852" is unsourced and conflates the census entry** — the 1907 application gives NO birth year and a different death year (1847, not 1852); "ca. 1831" is exactly the 1850 Lucy-19's implied birth year. The story's "contradiction stands" passage is now outdated and should be corrected on Rick's word (the sworn word was right all along) — **highest-priority correction.** (2026-10-01-patton-brown-dig.md; 2026-10-02-filing-lane-h-lucy-selby-evidence-2026-09-30.md)
2. **CORRECTION (verified) — the Selby theory is FALSIFIED.** The story theorizes: "it may be that Selby had married one of the Brown daughters — Margaret, Barbara, and Elizabeth are all gone from Thomas's household by 1860 — and that the twins, counted as infants in their grandparents' house in 1850, were his own children all along… That is a theory, not a fact, and the story marks it as one." Lane H **falsified** it: 1860 Cumberland, dwelling 208 — Greene B. Selby 49 KY; wife **"Teinpy" (Temperance), 43, b. ALABAMA**; Stephen BROWN 10 TN; Lucy BROWN 10 TN (twins keep the BROWN surname — **not Selby's children**). 1850 Bledsoe chain: Temperance Sherrell, 35, b. AL (dwelling 566, next to Selby's 563/564) → "Tempy" Selby, 43, b. ALA (1860) — Greene married Temperance Sherrell between 1850–1860. Alabama birthplace disqualifies any David W. Brown daughter (all b. TN). The story's theory passage needs rewriting on Rick's word. (2026-10-02-filing-lane-h-lucy-selby-evidence-2026-09-30.md)
3. **ADD (verified search) — Pompey Brown exists in exactly ONE record on earth.** Mary Catherine's sworn 1907 oath naming him is the ONLY place the name occurs in connection with this family — **no roll, census, book, or forum mentions him.** A **12,790-line Cherokee-Brown compilation was searched with zero hits.** Clean negatives: 1835 Henderson Roll (TN portion) — no "Pompey" anywhere; 1852 Chapman Roll — no Pompey, no Brown (geographic caveat — did not cover Bledsoe/Marion/Sequatchie); 1851 Siler Roll (same caveat); 1850 Bledsoe census all 60 Brown entries read ("Pomp" → zero hits); Emmett Starr, *History of the Cherokee Indians* (1921) full-text index. All other Pompey Browns ruled out (SC Jr. d. 1918; an enslaved Pompey in upstate SC; Pompey the Shawnee interpreter d. 1778; an Australian barber; a Black Loyalist). Graded: **unbacked-by-records — family testimony, not a proved ancestor.** The story presents Pompey via the oath (accurate as testimony); candidate: a clarifying line in the "note on what the records do and do not say" — "don't overstate him" (Rick's call). No Geni profile for Pompey exists; none made. (2026-10-01-pompey-brown.md; 2026-10-02-filing-lane-g-pompey-brown-report-2026-10-01.md)
4. **ADD nuance (verified census observation) — Dave arrived Bledsoe in the 1840s.** David W. Brown is **NOT in the 1840 Bledsoe census** — complete read of all 31 front sheets (NARA M704, Roll 517, 354 frames via Internet Archive; enumerated by Asst. Marshal James A. Tulloss, District No. 9); no household matches his profile (expected 9-person signature: David 41, wife 40–50, two daughters 10–15, etc.). The story's "By 1815, men named Brown were already paying tax in Bledsoe County… Which of them was Dave's father, or his uncle, no record says" is now sharpened: Dave wasn't there in 1840, so the 1815 Brown taxpayers (James ×2, John, Joseph, Samuel, Stephen) are weaker kin candidates than the story's framing suggests. One-line candidate. (2026-10-02-filing-dave-brown-1840-findings.md)
5. **Polly's maiden name — likely unrecoverable:** no deed gift, estate division, or will naming Polly/Mary Brown as a daughter found in any free source; most original Bledsoe will books were destroyed (TSLA microfilmed only the extant wills/inventories **1884–1931** — no pre-1884 Bledsoe will naming Polly); the only remaining lane is a TSLA microfilm/ILL pass. Dave's parents still unknown; whether Pompey was Dave's father or Polly's father unknown. (2026-10-02-filing-lane-f-polly-brown-maiden-name-report-2026-09-30.md)
**Lore (backup status):**
- Pompey Brown "full-blooded Cherokee" — **UNBACKED elsewhere, UNCONTRADICTED** — single-source (Mary's oath); do NOT imply roll absence disproves her oath. In story as testimony.
- J. M. Fletcher: his grandfather said Mary Catherine's ancestors were "of direct Indian descent" — third-hand oral-history chain preserved inside the federal packet — **UNBACKED as genealogy**.
- Walton's letters: "not later than the drop of the leaf" (his own idiom — verified nowhere else online); "hereditary Indian characteristics" as lineage argument — **VERIFIED as his wording, not as biology**.
- Cleo's dying testimony (Mary Catherine and both parents full Cherokee; raised Cherokee; knew her father; "both sides helped raise her") — **UNBACKED, uncontradicted**; "both sides helped raise her" fits the Brown reconstruction better than the book's "abandoned by father" framing. In story, held gently and labeled.
- Wilma + Cleo on *Orphan No More*: not fully accurate; the book's stories weren't the ones Mary passed down — **UNBACKED** oral history (book unlocatable).
- Wilma Crabtree Gibson's 1997/1999 queries (the Brown-line documentary backbone) — **SUPPORTED as compilation/leads, not proof**.
- Nonie Webb's compilation: a Thomas Brown holding 200 acres at Battle Creek adjoining George Lowry's Cherokee reservation (Marion Co. TN, 1824) — **context only, NOT kinship** (Dave's son Thomas not yet born in 1824; compiler filed it under "whites?").
- Identity firewalls: against Black Loyalist Pompey Brown, enslaved Pompey Browns, "Pompey Factor," and Cherokee leader James Brown's family — never conflate.
**PICTURES TO ADD:** none filed. Jesse W. Brown's 1924 death certificate (may name his parents and settle the nearby-kin lead) — still a lane, not a fact.
