## (5) Lawrence Lane × disputed widow identity — folder `lawrence-lane-disputed-widow-identity`

**Audit status:** story CLEAN ("The First Cabin"); deep dig 2026-10-02 found **no new findings**. Lore sweep items below postdate the main dig; the story carries the corroborated spine.

### FACTS TO ADD
- **Jacob's Revolutionary anecdotes, from his 1835 pension deposition (via Eric's Roots):** Jacob helped build fortifications and fascines at Morrisania; fought from about 11 a.m. to sundown at Morrisania, helped take a British fort, carrying off **two brass cannon and a swivel base**; on a later scouting action his outnumbered party **lay hidden in a marsh until dark while British soldiers fired over their heads**, one man shot in the heel. **LORE status: verified only as contents of Jacob's deposition through a cited secondary transcription** — the actual pension packet was not inspected and the events are not independently corroborated. Chapter flag: HIGH (most vivid first-person-ish material in Lawrence's whole circle; pairs poignantly with the pension *denial* for failure to prove six months' service). *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **Hardesty's exact wording (1883) for the 1808 cohort + "Grandfather Lane"** — quoted verbatim from wvgw.net/jackson/hardesty.html in the lore file; verified printed history; strengthens the Jacob-b.1737 resolution. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **Ravenswood town-naming legends (conflicting printed traditions — lore, not fact):** (1) the town was meant to honor "Ravensworth," the Fitzhugh family seat, but a map engraver miscopied it as Ravenswood; (2) the town was named for Lord Ravenswood of Walter Scott's *The Bride of Lammermoor*, which Fitzhugh family members were reading while choosing the name. Use both as community lore, flagged. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **Lawrence's Dutch origin confirmed:** Jacob Lane b. 5 July 1737 × Annetje Concklin m. 9 Jan 1770 Poughkeepsie; Laurens b. 1777 (DOCUMENTED parents); Mathijs/Anthonius Laen, b. ~1620 near Liège, sailed 1663 aboard the *De Rooseboom* ("The Rosetree") from Amsterdam to New Amsterdam — **the Mathijs-to-Jacob descent chain is unresolved by Eric's own account** ("the exact line of descent… seems yet to be determined"), so the 1663 ship detail stays lore. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **De Rooseboom tragedy detail:** Mathijs's wife Maryken Gijsberts died en route; families settled New Utrecht; Mathijs took the surname Lane, Anthonius took Van Pelt. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`

### LORE (labeled, backup status)
- **Delaware/Lenape displacement tale:** family moved after the Revolution to Delaware, driven out by Lenape attacks, losing everything including Jacob's discharge papers — **family narrative via a cited secondary (Eric's Roots, attributed to family sources); underlying records uninspected**. Presented in attribution, not as settled fact, without inherited 19th-century hostile phrasing. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **Defined negatives (actively hunted):** no obituary, anecdote, or authored work for Eleanor/Elizabeth/Nell Smith or Lawrence Lane anywhere on the open web; no lore for Lawrence's siblings (Henry/Henrik, Christina, John — only unrelated Lane lines); **no multiples** in Lawrence's circle; no securely identifiable first cousins (structural). *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`
- **Correction to an earlier track note:** the prior "no corroboration" verdict applied only to the spouse/Emily parentage claims — Lawrence's Dutch origin, the Lenape-displacement narrative, and the Ravenswood first-cabin/ejection history are corroborated to varying degrees. Don't carry "no corroboration" forward as if it applied to Lawrence himself. *Source:* `lore-lawrence-lane-nell-smith.md`

### PICTURES TO ADD
- **None filed.** `photos-manifest.md`: "No photos filed for this couple yet."

### UNKNOWNS
- The widow's identity: Elizabeth Smith (widow of Samuel Jackson, m. 1 Jan 1803) vs. "Eleanor 'Nell' Smith" — UNRESOLVED; leading hypothesis = conflation of Elizabeth Smith the widow with her daughter Eleanor "Nellie" Jackson; "Eleanor 'Nell' Smith, daughter of John Evans Smith × Letitia" = UNPROVED (no trace of John Evans Smith in any free source).
- Emily Lane (b. ~1817–1819) cannot be the widow's biological daughter (widow married Lawrence only after Nov 1821) — Emily's maternity disputed/unknown; story leaves parents blank responsibly.
- The 1849 Lawrence × Elizabeth Barnhart marriage: unresolved, two live hypotheses; groom's identity uncertain.
- Lawrence × Eleanor marriage record (~1800–1817, possibly Allegheny Co. PA): open. Jacob Lane's pension file (named children): open.

Samuel Doak (1749–1830)** — Presbyterian clergyman, founder of Washington College and Tusculum Academy (Greeneville, TN), Princeton 1775, member of the 1784–85 Franklin statehood convention, freed his slaves 1818 ("the apostle of learning and religion in the West"); plus Col. - **Marriage now DOCUMENTED: 14 Dec 1753, First Reformed Dutch Church, Poughkeepsie** ("Louwerens Conckelin and Annatje Schurri"; FamilySearch "New York Marriages, 1686-1980", FHL microfilm 974.7 B2N V. 73-75; via WikiTree Conklin-816) — the story reconstructed "~1753/1754" and left the registers as an open lane; the lane has been walked. John (Jan) Concklin** (b. abt 20 Aug 1700 Orange, NY; d. 15 Nov 1785 Poughkeepsie; husband of Annatje Storm; son of the Rye/Westchester Conklins) — commissioned Captain, Dutchess County Militia, September 1755; led a detachment to Albany in March 1757 for the relief of Fort William Henry (French and Indian War); signed the Poughkeepsie Articles of Association, 1775; deacon of the Poughkeepsie Dutch Reformed Church (1749, 1763, 1765). - New Hackensack Reformed Church records (Tower ed., Dutchess Co Historical Society Coll. Vol. V, p. 7): baptism 4 Oct 1757 "Laurens Conchling & Annatje Schurrig; Susanna, born Sep. 14" — record-level citation. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md; 2026-10-02-dig-conklin-deep.md)

Story: ch8.txt ll.3900–4153 ("John Richard Litton Sr. × Sarah Leighton", short ~2,300w). ### FACTS TO ADD
- **John Richard Litton Sr. SERVED (Rev War) — triple-checked:** (1) **DAR Patriot Ancestor A202089** — cited on his WikiTree profile: "Soldier John Litton Sr. served with Washington County Militia, Virginia Militia during the American Revolution"; (2) **Lewis Preston Summers, History of southwest Virginia, 1746–1786, roster p. 861: "Litton, John, King's Mountain"** — Washington Co. VA militia roster, King's Mountain campaign (1780); (3) **SAR lineage: U.S. Sons of the American Revolution Membership no. 90052** — descendant's accepted membership traces to him. The story's "no service" line must be corrected on Rick's word. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
- Rank caveat: WikiTree says "served as an officer (Lieutenant) in the Continental Army" but DAR/Summers point to **Virginia (Washington County) militia, not the Continental Line**; "Lieutenant" is compiled-only (Study Group chart: "A Lt. in Rev. War"; family account says he served as lieutenant as did all his brothers). (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)
- **James Fullen Litton family Bible (usgenwebsites.org/vagenweb/russell/vitals/bible/jflittonbible.html): "John R. LITTON, born March 1726 at Rockville, Maryland, and Sarah WILCOXEN, 1728, married 3 May 1745 at Fincastle, VA"** — names the couple directly; Bible gives birth as March 1726 (vs Jul 4 1726). 75%+. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-john-richard-litton.md)
- Son **Solomon Caleb Litton (b. 22 Dec 1751; d. 1843) SERVED — triple-checked:** Capt. John Duncan's Company, Washington County Militia, 26 Feb 1777–1784; commissioned Ensign 26 Feb 1777, Lieutenant 19 Aug 1778; also served at Glade Hollow Fort 29 Aug–6 Nov 1774 under Capt. Daniel Smith; **captured June 1780 with his family at John Martin's Station/Licking Creek by Shawnee under British Gen. Henry Bird; marched to Fort Detroit (Quebec), held until released by order of Gen. Haldimand 18 Jul 1783; his 1 Mar 1781 letter from "Shannee Town near ye Fort Detroit" survives in transcription**; son Solomon Jr. born at Fort Detroit just after the release. (sources: WikiTree Litton-200; Emory L. Hamilton manuscript pp. 63–65; frontierfolk.net transcription) (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md)

Hunter Price 27 Jun 1767); Elizabeth Opal Litton (b. 5 Jul 1749; m. John Laughlin 1 Apr 1765, s/o Thomas Laughlin); James Edward Litton (b. 13 Nov 1750; d. 10 May 1840 Williamsburg KY; **first Litton to have land surveyed in Washington Co, on Cedar Creek at Clinch Mt; built a log house there 1781**; m. Mary Ann Castle 29 Jun 1769, d/o Jacob Castle); Abraham Able Litton/Litten (b. 11 Jan 1780 Timberville VA; d. 5 Jun 1868 Montgomery Co VA). (source: 2026-10-02-dig-john-richard-litton.md)

Hall; Burton b. 1734 (moved to Abingdon, Washington Co VA); **James b. 1736** (d. ca. 1797 Lincoln Co NC; a private in the Revolution); Samuel b. 1738; **Michael b. 4-4-1740** (d. 1819; served in the American Revolution); Grace b. 12-31-1742; Grace Burton Litton m. 2nd Edward Willett (d. 1772 Frederick Co VA). ## COUPLE 5 — David D. Casto Jr. × Margaret Elizabeth Casto (P239)

Story: ch8.txt ll.4382–4857 ("The Open Attachment"). Grade in story: compiled, open, unproved; David Jr. b. Jun 1753 Turkey Point NJ, death conflict 23 Jan 1802 (profile) vs 1812 (brother's About); DAR A018952 "file behind it unopened"; Margaret née Provence b. 1756 "Greene" PA, d. 23 Jan 1802 Upshur, dau. of John William Provence Sr. × Elizabeth Thompson.

### FACTS TO ADD
- **David D. Casto Jr.'s service — DAR Ancestor #A018952 (VERIFIED DAR record): SOLDIER, Monongalia Co. VA / Washington Co. PA MILITIA** (Gwathmey, *Historical Register of Virginians in the Revolution*, p. 139; PA Archives, 6th Ser., Vol. 2, pp. 166, 172, 182). **CORRECTION: the story's guess "the likeliest shape is militia service: the Cumberland County militia" is wrong — it was the western frontier, NOT New Jersey.** County militia (no numbered regiment); the frontier war — Ohio Indian campaigns, blockhouses, the 1777–78 "bloody year," the Monongahela country's defense. Must NOT give him Continental battles. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **DAR children of David Jr. (application-level, 75%+):** John (m. Susanna McDade), **George [m. Elizabeth Westfall / Sarah Ours]** — the George with those wives IS George W. Casto, so DAR members have applied through him as David Jr.'s son — application-level support for the tree's attachment of George W. Casto to David Jr. × Margaret (the story's open parentage question); Phebe (m. Moses Bennett), David D (m. Frances Abbott). (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **Margaret's filiation — DAR A091908 (John William Provance): DAR applications list MARGARET [m. DAVID CASTO] among the children** — application-level support for the Provance→Margaret filiation. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **John William Provance's service — DAR A091908: PATRIOTIC SERVICE: "FURNISHED SUPPLIES; WIDOW SARAH PRESENTED RECEIPT MADE OUT TO HIM, 1783"** (Abercrombie & Slatten, *VA Rev Public Claims*, Vol. 2, p. 682) — civilian supply service, no battles, no unit; the Monongahela frontier's logistics. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **David Jr.'s death resolved by DAR: ANTE 10--1808, Harrison Co. VA (75%+)** — resolves the story's 1802-vs-1812 conflict in favor of the profile's 1802. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **Provence name etymology — correction (75%+): the story's "French in its sound... the Huguenots" framing is weakened.** Dictionary of American Family Names (via Geneanet): Provence = (1) English (of Norman origin), variant of Province — habitational from Provence, France; (2) probably also an altered form of Provance, hence a variant of **Purviance**, of ultimately Scottish origin. The Purviance/Scottish line is stronger ("The majority of the Provines trace their origin to John Provine born 1751 in Lancaster County, PA... son of William Purviance, who came to North America as a child from Ireland"). The French-Huguenot hint is below-75%. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-casto-jr-expanded.md)
- Provance's two families: wife #1 **Elizabeth Thomson (Thompson)** — Margaret's likely mother; wife #2 **Sarah Yards Wright** (the widow who presented his 1783 supply receipt) — her son Joseph Yards (new collateral lane). Which wife bore Margaret is open. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-casto-jr-expanded.md)
- George W. Casto's public offices — VERIFIED via the story's two published histories: school commissioner 1831, JP, sheriff 1835, assessor 1841; d. 19 Apr 1845, will probated 27 Oct 1845. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-casto-jr-expanded.md)
- Book sweep: NEGATIVE for the couple (Stanley D. Casto's 1979 *Descendants of David D. Casto and Rachel Boice* covers a LATER namesake, not this couple). (source: book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

### FLAGS (not story-adds — adjudication for Rick)
- **Provance birth/death discrepancies:** DAR gives John William Provance as b. **CIRCA 1730** (story: 30 Nov 1720, New London, Chester Co. PA) and d. **ANTE 13 May 1783, Monongalia Co. VA** (story: 1782, Fayette Co. PA, McClain Graveyard). Story's dates are contradicted by DAR — do not narrate the 1720/McClain version as fact. (source: 2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- "Margaret's 1756 Greene PA" birth is anachronistic (Greene formed 1796 from Washington). (source: 2026-10-02-dig-casto-jr-expanded.md)

## (3) Samuel Bonnett Sr. × Mary Elizabeth Bonnett (story ~l.5563–5920)

### FACTS TO ADD
1. IMMIGRANT SHIP FOUND: the **Elizabeth** (Rotterdam → Philadelphia, arrived **27 August 1733**) carried Samuel's parents Jean Jacques (Jacob) Bonnet × Anna Marie Desreux — Strassburger & Hinke *Pennsylvania German Pioneers* List 30A (Captain's List): "Jacob Bunnet, age 32, with wife Mary 32, children Margret 8; Susanna 4, died en route; Christina 2, died en route; and John Simon 3/4." Two daughters died on the crossing. The story currently says "the entry gives no emigration... the telling does not build on sound." (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`)
2. Jean Jacques's Friedrichstal records: confirmed 9 May 1717 ("Jaques Bonet fils de Daniel Bonet," aged ~15); Friedrichstal was founded 1699 by the Margrave of Baden-Durlach specifically for Huguenot refugees; the family came from Billigheim and vicinity (arrived Jan 1700, first housed at Spöck). Anna Marie Desreux: b. ~1701/1702, daughter of Abraham "Ancien" Desreux × Judith Guerit; young widow of Jean Corbeau; m. Jean Jacques 19 Oct 1723 at St. Evangile Church, Friedrichstal. (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`)
3. Jacob Bonnet's land: 400 acres "Tulpehocken on the Schuylkill" surveyed 15 Dec 1737 (PA Archives 3rd ser. vol. 54 p. 358); by ~1741 in Monocacy Valley, MD ("Wine Garden" certificate 28 May 1742, assigned to Martin Wetzel; "Bonnett's Resolution" patented 1743). The Bonnet and Wetzel families met in Chester County PA and moved to Maryland together. (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`)
4. Samuel's migration/land: 1766 bought 205 acres on Cedar Creek, Hardy Co. (then Hampshire) VA; 1777 leased the Cedar Creek land to David Myles; 1784 Hampshire Co. VA census; 1786 bought 400 acres in Monongalia Co. (later Harrison); killed fall 1789 on Hackers Creek, nine children still at home. (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`)
5. The 12-child roster (Don Norman's "Descendants of John Bonnett," published genealogy — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION; frontier.com GEDCOM): Margaret (1759–1792) m. John Waggoner; John (1756–1783); Henry (1761–1799) m. Elizabeth Lorentz; Jacob (1762–1847) m. Martha Hughes; Lewis (1762–1850) m. Mary Linger; Peter (1764–1835) m. Margaret Linger; Samuel (1770–1840) m. Martha Radcliff; Elizabeth (1772–1824) m. George Straley; William (1774–1858) m. Barbara Harpold; Philip (1775–1860) m. Susan Linger; Mary (1777–1845) m. Peter Flesher; Susannah (1778–1858) m. Christian Straley. The story's "Children" section names only Hannah and says the others are "not established." Propose the roster as the compiled/published account, with the Hannah-vs-Elizabeth conflict flagged alongside. (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`, `book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
6. TWINS — Jacob & Lewis Bonnett (both b. 1762): Don Norman — "Jacob Bonnett... a twin of Lewis Bonnett"; Lewis's pension narrative: "His twin brother was Jacob, aged 75." The story's Multiples section says "no twins... the family's twins come on other lines" — OUTDATED. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`, `2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
7. Sons Lewis, Jacob, and Peter served as **Indian spies on the western frontier** (Virginia Line), enlisted Mar 1779/80, served to 1783; pensions S5294 (Lewis, applied 5 Feb 1833, age 71) and S5293 (Peter, applied 5 Feb 1833, age 69) — SUPPORTED (pension abstracts, two published references). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
8. Pension scandal — lore, CLAIMED (compiled): all three applied for pensions, but "brother Samuel testified that they were too young to have served and hence the pensions were revoked"; Lewis's certificate suspended 1833 "as he had not been in the regular service." Label as family tale/lore; the pension files themselves are the primary (NARA/Fold3, paywalled). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
9. Son John Bonnett (1757–Sep 1783) killed by Indians April 1783 while pursuing Indians, "buried in a cave in Lewis City, Virginia" — lore, CLAIMED (two compiled sources). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
10. Daughter Margaret (1759–1792) killed by Indians May 7, 1792 in the Tecumseh raid — lore, from Don Norman's published genealogy. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
11. Daughter Mary Bonnett m. Peter Flesher Jun 28 1793 (Don Norman) — cross-family link to the Flesher line. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
12. Published books: Don Norman, "Descendants of John Bonnett" (Internet Archive item norman003) — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION (full entry: vitals, marriage, 12 children); plus *The Bonnet-t-e's & Kin*, Vol. 18 (1994). (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
13. John Lorence's will (signed 14 June 1817, proved 8 July 1817, Hardy County VA) — the Lorence family in Hardy County (same county as Samuel's 1766 purchase); the will's daughter "Elizabeth Bonnett" received $100 (a later generation, but confirms Bonnett–Lorence intermarriage in the county). (`2026-10-02-dig-bonnett-family.md`)

## (4) Henry Flesher × Elizabeth Susannah Flesher (story ~l.5921–6204)

### FACTS TO ADD
1. Published book (SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION): Edward Conrad Smith, *A History of Lewis County, West Virginia* (1920), pp. 73–74 — the October 1784 Indian attack: Henry was hauling logs for a stable when fired on by an Indian lying in wait; the ball passed through his arm; "Mrs. Flesher" pulled him into the house and scared the attacker away by calling on the other men to fire; tradition says she carried him into a thicket near the house and spent the night under a sycamore at the mouth of Stone Coal Creek. Smith calls him "Henry Flesher, the pioneer of Weston" and "called a captain in the traditional account." Lore with backup status (published 1920 county history; the sycamore night labeled tradition). Not in the story. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
2. Military identity, with the stated caveat: Henry "Weston" Flesher (12 Nov 1734 Ober-Seemen, Hessen – Feb 1803 Weston, Lewis Co.) — enlisted private in Capt. Michael Bowyer's Company, 12th Virginia Regiment, 7 Aug 1776; re-enlisted 6 Mar 1777 for two years; promoted Sergeant 21 Aug 1777 (pay $8/month); discharged 19 Aug 1778; commissioned 2nd Lieutenant in Capt. John McCoy's Company of the Virginia Militia; qualified as Ensign 10 June 1781 (Chalkley's Augusta County Records, Vol. 1); appointed Major of the Militia in Pendleton County, Dec 1788; company muster/pay rolls show "Sergt." Henry Flesher at **Valley Forge, winter 1777–78** (Hacker's Creek Journal); SAR Patriot P-159321; Find a Grave memorial 87685362 ("Lieut Henry 'Heinrich' Flesher — Revolutionary War service"). The story gives Henry "no dates, no places, no trade, no more." IDENTITY CAVEAT: Hacker's Creek Journal Vol. 6 distinguishes the Weston/Lewis County pioneer Henry (died before the pension/bounty-land era; "nothing has been found in public records to support" a Continental-service claim for him) from Major Henry Flesher of Pendleton County (the Continental Line sergeant — deposition 1 Feb 1820 supporting John Slaven's pension; 200-acre VA land warrant as "serjeant in the Continental line" issued 23 Sept 1809). The compiled identity merges the two men — CLAIMED, not primary-proved. (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
3. WeRelate: m. 1759 **Elizabeth Bush** (abt.1742–1807), daughter of George Adam and Catherine Stump Bush — vs the story's "no maiden name... whether she was born a Flesher or married into the name." The folder's "Elizabeth Susannah" = Bush (consistent, not contradictory). CLAIMED (compiled: email from Jeanne Wurmser PhD + Rootsweb chart). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
4. Anna Elizabeth "Nancy" Flesher (1761–abt.1850) listed as Henry × Elizabeth Bush's FIRST child (WeRelate) — matches the folder's "Anne = Nancy Ann Flesher" and the c.1780 Shiflett marriage chronology (married at ~19). CLAIMED. (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
5. Son Adam Flesher (1764–1854) — "served as a scout and ranger against the northwestern Indians" (SAR GJB page; CLAIMED, single compiled source). (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
6. Harry E. Flesher bio (usgwarchives, Taylor Co.): "Henry Flesher was a soldier in the Revolutionary war... located a grant of eight hundred acres in Lewis County... shot by Indians, but recovered." (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
7. Related lead (journal article, not a book): Walter Farewell, "Biographical Sketch of Henry Flesher," *Hacker's Creek Journal* VI, no. 3 (1988): 156–185 — a 30-page sketch. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)

## (5b) Maj. William Haymond Sr. × Cassandra Haymond — same combined gated section (story ~l.6205–6495; Haymond half ~l.6390–6445)

### FACTS TO ADD
1. Cassandra's maiden name: **CLELLAND** — b. 25 Oct 1741, Prince George's Co MD, daughter of Thomas × Jane Cleland; m. William 19 Apr 1763; d. 23 Dec 1788. The story says "No maiden name is given in the sources read" — OUTDATED. Fresh bloodline-adjacent lead: the Clelands of Prince George's Co MD (MD church/probate/land records unsearched). (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`, `2026-10-02-audit-maj-william-haymond-sr-cassandra-haymond.md`)
2. French and Indian War service (Henry Haymond's 1910 county history, p. 369 — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION book): **Braddock's expedition 1755** at age 15 — accompanied Gen. Edward Braddock's march to capture Fort Duquesne, survived the **Battle of the Monongahela, 9 Jul 1755** (family tradition: "likely in the quarter-master's department," labeled tradition); **Forbes expedition 1758**; enlisted Feb 1759 in the **Virginia Regiment under Col. George Washington** — garrisoned the captured country along the Monongahela and Allegheny Rivers and as far north as Presque Isle (Erie, PA), then marched up the Shenandoah Valley to the Holston River to suppress a Cherokee outbreak; discharged at **Fort Lewis, near Staunton, VA, 24 Feb 1762** — discharge states he "duly served three years and behaved as a good soldier and faithful subject." None of it is in the story. (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-military-deepdive.md`)
3. Exact Revolutionary commissions (state-document level — Saffell's *Revolutionary Records*, pp. 267/256): **Captain, Monongalia County Militia — commissioned by Gov. Patrick Henry, 13 Mar 1776**; **in command of Prickett's Fort on the Monongahela, 1777**, with a detachment at Scott's Mills; **May 1777** — selected as one of the officials administering the oath renouncing allegiance to George III to Monongalia Co males 16+; served 1776 through the close of the war; **Major of Militia — commissioned by Gov. Benjamin Harrison, 12 Nov 1781**; **Sheriff of Monongalia County, 1783**. This upgrades the story's fort command from lineage-society lead to state-document level (the story's caution stands, but the lead is much stronger). (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-military-deepdive.md`)
4. Fort spelling correction: **Prickett's Fort** (built 1774 during Dunmore's War on Jacob Prickett's land; refuge for ~80 families; never attacked) — the story writes "Pickett's Fort" following its DAR citation; the histories spell it Prickett's. (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-military-deepdive.md`, audit)
5. Post-war: principal surveyor of Harrison Co 1784 (examined at William & Mary), JP, deputy surveyor; estate appraisal 9 Mar 1822 at WVRHC (compass and chain, "Virginia Justice"). (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`)
6. Parents: **John Haymond** (came from England prior to 1734) × **Margaret Calder** (m. 22 Aug 1723, Queen Anne's Parish MD); siblings Calder, Nicholas. (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`)
7. The move: sold the Rockville farm May 1773 to James Sutter (£212 10s); moved with wife, four children, and enslaved laborers to the District of West Augusta, VA, on the Monongahela near present Morgantown — "a howling wilderness." (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`)
8. William remarried Mary "Polly" Pettyjohn Powers, 29 Dec 1789; children Cyrus, Ruth, Maxa (half-siblings of Margaret). (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`)
9. Published book (SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION): Henry Haymond, *History of Harrison County, West Virginia* (1910), p. 369 — "Major William Haymond" in "Sketches of Pioneers." Related-negative: William C. Haymond, *Record of the Haymond Family* EXCLUDES this couple's line (devoted to Edward Haymond × Milly Calder descendants). (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
10. ANCESTOR-AUTHORED lead: "William Haymond's Letters" printed in the 1910 Harrison County history (p. 352, TOC-verified) — content not yet opened; priority lead for the digs. (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
11. Kin lead: Edward Haymond (nephew — "Uncle William") served with the militia at Prickett's Fort 1774–76; enlisted 26 Aug 1776 as a regular under Capt. John Wilson, 8th PA Regiment, Col. McCoy's Pennsylvania Line. (`2026-10-02-dig-haymond-family.md`)

## 2. Jacob Reitenauer × Susanna Barbara Semple — P259 (8th greats)
Story: "The Maryland Germans" (ch8.txt ~lines 6827–7145). Verdicts match research (supported marriage; DAR A134131 fenced; attached parents unverified; Easterday tier carried as attached). **The story says "There is no emigration tale" for the Reitenauers — there now IS a documented emigration.**

### FACTS TO ADD
1. THE SHIP: "Nicholas Reitenhower" (Nicholas Reitenauer II, b. ~1683 Tieffenbach, Alsace) aboard the *Robert & Alice*, of Dublin, Rotterdam → Philadelphia, qualified 3 Sept 1739 (List 71 A; master Walt. Goodman; 78 men 16+, 218 souls, Palatines). Ship/date/list/captain VERIFIED (Strassburger & Hinke, *Pennsylvania German Pioneers* Vol. I; Olive Tree Genealogy transcription); Nicholas II identity 75%+ (compiled genealogies); Nicholas II → Mathias (bp. 25 Oct 1725) → Jacob chain 75%+ compiled. Jacob (b. 1759 MD) was born in America — the immigrant is his grandfather. (`jacob-reitenauer-susanna-barbara-semple/2026-10-02-dig-reitenauer-ship-and-siblings.md`; `2026-10-02-deepdig-nicholas-1739-ship.md`; `documents/robert-and-alice-1739-passenger-list-NOTE.txt`)
2. Independent corroboration: Rupp, *Thirty Thousand Names* (1876) gives the same man, ship, and date. (`jacob-reitenauer-susanna-barbara-semple/book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
3. Jacob's siblings — children of Mathias × Eve (paulridenour.com tree): John Nicholas (1755–1845), Henry (1757–1810), Jacob (1759–1827), John (1762–1801), Eva (1764–), Rosina (1766–), Mathias Jr. (1770–1803), Daniel (1773–), David (1775–1854) — 75%+ as a roster; individual dates below bar. WARNING: this David (1775–1854) is NOT our David (b. 1793) — likely uncle/cousin; do not merge. (`2026-10-02-dig-reitenauer-ship-and-siblings.md`)
4. 1743 patent: "Nicholas Ridenour, of Prince George County," 100 acres "Ridenours Pond" near Conocoheague — the immigrant's land. (same)
5. Related sailings, same kin group: *Robert & Alice* 11 Sept 1738 — "Hans Henrich Reitenaur," "Peter Reittenauer"; *Lydia* 11 Dec 1739 — "Johannes Reytenaar" 40 and "Johannes Reytenaar, Jun'r" 16. (`2026-10-02-deepdig-nicholas-1739-ship.md`)
6. Peter D. Ridenour's 1908 *Autobiography* describes Nicholas as "born in 1695 in the Lower Rhine of the Palatinate, Germany, and immigrated to Washington Co., Maryland in 1739" (m. Rosina Kershner, d. ~1760) — ancestor-authored, compiled. (same)
7. Hagerstown named for John Jager, a brother-in-law of Nicholas Ridenour — color. (`2026-10-02-dig-reitenauer-ship-and-siblings.md`)
8. Geni DAR data on Jacob's profile (curator Jim Wile): rank PRIVATE — "Capt John Kershner guarding prisoners; Capt Jacob Good" — the profile's claimed detail, no second source; the "guarding prisoners" detail suggests Maryland militia prisoner-guard duty at Frederick Barracks — LEAD, below bar. (`2026-10-02-p259-verification.md`; `2026-10-02-dig-reitenauer-ship-and-siblings.md`)
9. DAR-claim conflation guard: John "R" Ridenour (b. ~1740/45 MD; d. 1828 Campbell Co TN) has documented Rev War service — a DIFFERENT man from our Jacob (b. 11 Jan 1759, d. 21 Sep 1827 Frederick Co MD); any "Jacob Ridenour, Revolutionary War" hit must be checked against these identities. (`2026-10-02-dig-military-sweep.md`)
10. Geneanet Dowling tree: Mathias Ridenour (1725–1792) × Anna Eva Waldi → Jacob 1759–1827 × Susanna Stemple (m. ~1780), 10 children — no military claims attached. (same)

## 4. Thomas Copley II × Mary Day — P268 (8th greats)
Story: "The Yorkshire Crossing" (ch8.txt ~lines 7438–7680). The story already carries the Mary Day identity resolution ("'Mary Copley' is Mary Day under her married name") and the DAR A132358 flag — but only the flag, not the service. NOTE: the folder audit (2026-10-02) compared against the Ch7 kid's story ("The Copley Sisters"); the findings below were re-checked against the Ch8 story itself.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. MILITARY — Battle of Point Pleasant, **10 Oct 1774**, Lord Dunmore's War: **Capt. John Lucas's Company, Col. William Christian's Fincastle regiment, Col. Andrew Lewis's southern wing** — Shawnee chief Cornstalk's attack on ~1,000 Virginians at the Kanawha-Ohio confluence; day-long battle; ~75 Virginians killed, ~140 wounded; Virginians marched on with Dunmore's northern force and forced the Treaty of Camp Charlotte, ending Dunmore's War. 75%+ (Summers, *Annals of Southwest Virginia* p. 1423 via WikiTree Copley-269; SAR recognizes Point Pleasant service as Revolutionary service). The story carries only the DAR flag. (`thomas-copley-ii-mary-day/2026-10-02-dig-copley-military-immigration.md`)
2. Revolutionary War: **Capt. Daniel Smith's Company of Fincastle Soldiers**, Montgomery County militia (post-1776 Fincastle division) — 75%+ (DAR A132358). (same)
3. Date correction: the family tradition's "about 1759" is WRONG — Point Pleasant was 1774 (Thomas was ~38, not 23); "May 30, 1774" was the muster date, not the battle date. (same)
4. Capt. Daniel Smith = likely the surveyor/senator (1748–1818; at Guilford Courthouse and Kings Mountain; brigadier general; U.S. Senator 1798–99, 1805–09) — 75% (same name/county/war), not proved. (same)
5. **TWINS: James and Joshua Copley, both born 18 Apr 1775** — the multiples flag; the story's "Multiples: none documented" is outdated. (`2026-10-02-audit-thomas-copley-ii-mary-day.md`)
6. The New River ferry below Spruce Run: tolls 6 1/4 cents/man/horse/cattle, ~1.56 cents/hog-sheep-goat, 9 1/2 cents/wheel for wagon/cart. (same)
7. Thomas applied to open a road from his canoe landing to the Court House. (same)
8. 25 Jan 1783 Commissioner's Certificate: 160 acres settled 1772, surveyed 4 Feb 1775, west side of New River. (same)
9. 1773 delinquent list (James McGavock); tax rolls: 1 tithable, 3 horses, one slave 16+, 80 cents tax; sons James, John, Joshua, Joseph on the same rolls. (same)
10. 1810 census (Montgomery Co.): 1 male 0-10, 1 male 1 female 45+. (same)
11. UPDATE: the Geni tree HAS been corrected — the profile now reads "Mary Copley (Day)" (verified live 2026-10-02); the story's "The tree has not been corrected" is outdated. (`2026-10-02-geni-mary-day-verified.md`)
12. Book-mentions NEGATIVE; Miller's *History of Summers County* (1908) verified clean of the couple by full-text search; the Summers *Annals* p. 1423 citation is unverified (lending-restricted). (`book-mentions-2026-10-02.md`)
- BELOW BAR (flag only, never propose): the "Charming Molly" 1764 ship tradition + eldest son Joshua "born aboard ship" — UNVERIFIED, stays out; the WikiTree Plantagenet royal lineage (Edward I → Thomas Copley) — UNBACKED, flag, do not carry; the Find a Grave Mary Day Copley memorial number conflict (43537859 vs 43537807) — needs a live check; the Halifax-vs-Leckhamstead birthplace conflict (possible baptism 12 Sep 1737 Wheldrake, Yorkshire) — below 75%.
- SMALL CONSISTENCY NOTE: the story carries Thomas's death as "1821 in Boone" per the tree; the research carries the 1818 will proved Jan 1822 in Giles Co. — the audit did not flag this; flagging for the file crew, not proposed as a story add.
- KID-STORY CONSISTENCY: the Ch7 "Copley Sisters" story carries the Mary Day discrepancy as "unresolved"; the 2026-10-02 identity resolution ("Mary Copley" = Mary Day, 85%; identity report filed in documents/) supersedes it — kid-story business, flagged for continuity.

## 2. Josiah Meador × Lavinia Moody (P280) — folder `josiah-meador-lavinia-moody/`

Story: Ch8 "The Threefold Braid" + "The Bristol Parish Moodys" + "The grandchildren's yards" (~3,800 words). Audit 2026-10-02: MATCHES research. Voice flag: "the telling" used 46× (same class as the ch7 "the chapter" meta-device — Rick's call whether to change).

### FACTS TO ADD
1. The son's unit's exact designation: the Illinois Regiment of Virginia Volunteers (Illinois Regiment, Virginia State Troops) — VIRGINIA STATE LINE, not the Continental Army. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-military-deepdive.md)
2. The authorization chain: authorized January 1778 by Governor Patrick Henry; Clark promoted to lieutenant colonel Jan 2, 1778; mustered ~150–175 men at Old Redstone Fort (vic. Brownsville, PA), departed by flatboat down the Monongahela/Ohio May 12, 1778, rendezvoused at the Falls of the Ohio (Corn Island, vic. Louisville KY) May 27, 1778; regiment disbanded 1784. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-military-deepdive.md)
3. Fort Donnally, May 29, 1778: ~200 Wyandot/Mingo attacked the fort (built 1767 by Col. Andrew Donnally); garrison 25–29 militiamen under Lt. Col. Andrew Donnally, Capt. William McCoy, Lt. John Williams; relief ~3 p.m. by ~66–67 militiamen from Fort Savannah (Lewisburg) under Col. Samuel Lewis, Capt. Matthew Arbuckle, Capt. John Stuart; casualties: 17 Indian dead, 4 militia/settler dead (John Prichet, Alexander Ochiltree, James Burns, James Graham), 2 wounded. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-military-deepdive.md)
4. Fort Jefferson (May 1780–June 1781, built at the Ohio-Mississippi confluence) and the June 1781 discharge at the Falls of the Ohio after the fort was abandoned — the story's battle list ends without the discharge. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-military-deepdive.md)
5. The company-commander name variant, unresolved: the pension says Captain Joseph Renfroe; Miller 1908 says Captain Joseph Henfoe (Jarrett's Fort on Wolf Creek, Keeney's Fort on the Greenbrier), then re-enlisted under Captain Isaac Taylor, Colonel James Montgomery's Regiment — flagged, not resolved; the pension file (S7225, login-walled) would settle it. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-military-deepdive.md)
6. Miller 1908 verified quote for the church section: "The Bluestone Baptist Church, now located at Jumping Branch, was organized in the year 1798, in the house of Rev. Josiah Meador, on Little Bluestone River. The house was a double log cabin, with a dirt floor, where the people met to worship for several years" (p. 324) — verified from the Internet Archive full text (identifier `historyofsummers00mill`); SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION of the couple's son. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
7. The Cherokee-descent lore hunt was re-run 2026-10-02 with no new sources: no rolls, applications, or sworn statements on the public web — status stays UNVERIFIED/unbacked, exactly as the story labels it; no change to the story's labeling. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-deep.md)

### FLAGGED (not proposed)
- Lavinia Moody parentage lead: Find a Grave memorial 86134177 (Walter Pack Family) gives her christening 3/12/1731, Bristol Parish, parents Robert Moody × Ann Preston, citing Hatcher's 1968 genealogy — compiled, UNPROVED; the story says her parents are unknown in searched sources. (2026-10-02-dig-meador-deep.md)
- Date conflict inside the folder: book-mentions header gives Josiah b. ~1736 Caroline Co / d. 1795–1798 and Lavinia b. 12 Mar 1730 Bristol Parish / d. aft. 22 Aug 1798 — against the dig's 3/12/1731 christening; all compiled. The story honestly carries no birth/death years proved. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md; 2026-10-02-dig-meador-deep.md)
- The 7 Jan 1793 Botetourt deed (DB 4 p.445, Arthur Cooper → Robert Lilly, 100 acres on Back Creek, "the place whereon Josiah Meadows deceased lived"; witnesses Josiah Meadows II, Jacob Meadows, Robert Richardson) — RECORD-level lead, the deed book itself unopened (FamilySearch login lane); suggests TWO Josiahs conflated (Josiah I of Back Creek, d. <1793, vs Josiah 1738–1836). ## 3. William Cannaday × Nancy Hill (P283/P287) — folder `william-cannaday-nancy-hill/`

Story: Ch8 "The Two James Cannadays" (William Cannaday × Nancy Hill). Audit 2026-10-02: MATCHES research; the identity conflict (research's James vs the tree's James; Nancy Hill vs Nancy Cannaday; two Williams, two Nancys) is documented exactly as the folder carries it. Kid-story consistent. Voice clean.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. James Cannaday's service window (Oct 1776–1 Mar 1777) covers exactly TRENTON (26 Dec 1776 — the 3rd Virginia, 181 men under Col. Weedon, in the order of battle) and PRINCETON (3 Jan 1777 — Col. Mercer killed, Weedon succeeded to brigade command and promotion) — the story proves the service but names no battle he WAS at. (2026-10-02-military-james-cannaday-3rd-virginia.md)
2. After Princeton: wintered with the army at Morristown, New Jersey (Jan–Mar 1777), discharged 1 March 1777; he missed Brandywine, Germantown, Monmouth, and Charleston (all after his discharge). (2026-10-02-military-james-cannaday-3rd-virginia.md)
3. The DAR record's exact service description for A064724: "CAPT WALLACE, COL WEEDON" — private in Captain Wallace's company, Colonel William Weedon's 3rd Virginia Regiment, Continental Line; service source NARA M881, Compiled Military Service Records, Roll #952 (free NARA-catalog lane). (charles-raikes-nancy-lovatt-raikes/2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
4. DAR residence entries: Buckingham Co. VA and Henry Co. VA — migration-path material for the Cannaday line (death: before 3 Mar 1817, Franklin Co VA, consistent with the 1817 will the story cites). (charles-raikes-nancy-lovatt-raikes/2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
5. DAR applications corroborate the children's roster: William (m. Martha Patsy Wright), James (m. Sarah Young), Pleasant (m. Elizabeth Young), Mary Polly (m. Pleasant Thomas) — matching the story's carried children. (charles-raikes-nancy-lovatt-raikes/2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
6. The 2007 compiled family history "Early Canaday/Cannady/Etc Families And their Arrival in America" (verified from the actual PDF text): "William Canaday born October 16, 1730. He was first married about 1750, but we don't have her name. He later married Nancy Hill September 11, 1759 and ended up in Franklin County, Virginia" — CAVEAT: self-published web compilation (2007), explicitly labeled "guesswork" by its compiler; still the only located genealogy naming the William × Nancy HILL couple with a marriage date and the Franklin Co VA migration. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
7. The Kennedy → Cannaday name-change lore has linguistic legs: Kennedy variants include Kannady, Kennaday, Canaday (Scotland: Ayrshire/Galloway; "Canaday (England)" listed as a variant) — fits the "possibly from Ireland, probably via England" immigrant claim (Ulster Scots often staged through England); still lore, not baseless. (2026-10-02-audit-william-cannaday-nancy-hill.md)

### FLAGGED (not proposed)
- "History of the Cannaday family of Virginia" (Frieda E. & Walter Clark Cannaday, Seminole FL, c1983, 2 vols., ill., maps, geneal. tables — FamilySearch catalog koha:311972) — UNOPENED; the catalog's own summary states: "James Cannaday I (ca.1755-1817), son of William and Nancy Cannaday, immigrated (possibly from Ireland, probably via England) to Patrick County, Virginia and married Elizabeth Raikes (a fellow passenger)" — three bombs: parents given as "William and Nancy CANNADAY" not Hill (supports the tree's attachment against the research roster); an IMMIGRANT origin; Elizabeth as a FELLOW PASSENGER (ship unnamed). Compiled, book unopened — UNBACKED until read. (2026-10-02-audit-william-cannaday-nancy-hill.md)
- Second Canaday cluster: John Canaday (b. bef 1722, d. 3 Jun 1773 Hanover Parish, King George Co VA; will King George Will Bk. A, pp. 337–338), m. Susannah Triplett bef 1742; son of John Cannaday (1707–1753) × Margaret Summers (m. 17 Aug 1727, St. Peters Parish, Talbot Co, MD) — chase-or-rule-out (Bill Putman 2007 article; WikiTree Cannaday-12). (2026-10-02-audit-william-cannaday-nancy-hill.md)
- Western branch: William Cannady Sr. of Nelson Co KY (b. 1767 per 1850 census; bought 150 acres Nelson Co 14 Jun 1791; sold 1801; moved to Vermilion Co IL May 1828; m. Rebecca Watson 1792, daughter of Henry Watson) — chase-or-rule-out. (2026-10-02-audit-william-cannaday-nancy-hill.md)

## 4. Charles Raikes × Nancy Lovatt Raikes (P284/P288) — folder `charles-raikes-nancy-lovatt-raikes/`

Story: Ch8 "Betty, Wife of the Patriot: Charles Raikes × Nancy Lovatt Raikes" (~3,800 words). Audit 2026-10-02: MATCHES research; verdict UNPROVED-ATTACHED carried; every conflict preserved (birth 1765 vs c.1755; death 1834 vs 1 Sep 1853 "lived to be 105"; 1787 bond vs 1775; "married at twelve" lore unbacked). Voice clean (zero hits on banned phrases). P288 is a collateral duplicate of P284.

### FACTS TO ADD
1. The DAR record's exact service description for ancestor A064724: "CAPT WALLACE, COL WEEDON" — private in Captain Wallace's company, Colonel William Weedon's 3rd Virginia Regiment, Continental Line; service source NARA M881, Compiled Military Service Records, Roll #952 (free NARA-catalog lane) — the story says "the private of the Third Virginia" but gives no company/regiment commanders. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
2. DAR residence entries for James Cannaday: Buckingham Co. VA and Henry Co. VA — migration-path material (death before 3 Mar 1817, Franklin Co VA). (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)

4. Typical-ship account (TYPICAL, labeled): an English emigrant of the 1760s–1770s typically crossed on a London- or Bristol-out merchantman bound for Virginia or Philadelphia — the pre-Revolutionary emigration was dominated by indentured servants (convict transport ended 1776; servant traffic continued); 6–10 week crossing, between-decks berths; no evidence whether Charles Raikes came as a servant or a freeman — both were common. (2026-10-02-colonial-raikes-crossing.md)
5. A RAIKES surname vertical file exists at the Virginia-North Carolina Piedmont Genealogical Society (per the DAR bible-record index) — not a book, contents unknown/unopened. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

## 5. Daniel Shumate × Millicent "Milly" Callison [attached, unproved] (P293) — folder `daniel-shumate-millicent-shumate/`

Story: Ch8 "Daniel Shumate × Millicent 'Milly' Callison [attached, unproved]" — a collateral-branch retelling of the Ch7 Tollison story's parent section, ending at Tollison's birth (27 Feb 1775); the post-1775 years (ensign's commission, Rich Creek 1780, eleven children, the 1826 deaths) belong to the later telling. Folder verdict: UNPROVED-ATTACHED (both Master Profiles, Ben M. Angel — Geni read-only). Audit 2026-10-02: matches research EXCEPT one material contradiction on Tollison's dates (below).

### FACTS TO ADD
1. "Founder of Rich Creek" — VERIFIED by the Town of Rich Creek's official history page: "Daniel Shumate and Milly Callison with their three sons settled Rich Creek and built their first log home in 1780" — belongs to the LATER telling (the Ch8 prequel ends 1775; the story itself says the Rich Creek move belongs there). (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
2. The 1787 land grant of 387 acres matches the documented land-grant record; the second log home "Riverside" stayed "home of the Hardin Shumates" until sold to Emory and Louise Eaton in 1981 — within living memory; the house may still stand — LATER telling. (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
3. Millicent's photographed stone: Find a Grave memorial 5920849, Sunrise Memorial Gardens, Rich Creek, Giles Co VA — "5 Oct 1758 – 22 May 1841"; the stone is preferred over USGenWeb's "11 Sept 1841" — CONFLICTS with the Ch8 story's compiled "born in 1753 and dead in 1826": stone-level evidence, adjudication needed before the edit pass. (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
4. Mark Hardin Shumate (b. 1 Dec 1783, d. 22 Feb 1873) × Elizabeth Leach (d. 3 Feb 1873) — the "Riverside" home line; husband and wife died weeks apart — compiled (grandchild-line beat). (2026-10-02-dig-shumate-expanded.md)
5. Oren F. Morton's *History of Monroe County, West Virginia* (1916, free via archive.org) militia roster: "Daniel Shumate — Lieutenant — 1784; Tolison Shumate — Lieutenant under McDaniel — 1799; resigned 1801; Harden Shumate — Lieutenant — 1815" — verified secondary; confirms the family's militia-officer pattern but does NOT settle which Daniel did Revolutionary service. (2026-10-02-dig-shumate-expanded.md)
6. Slavery in the Fauquier kin circle — VERIFIED (will abstract + deed excerpt; "chapter must include slavery honestly"): John Shumate II's 1783 will names an enslaved person "Nell" (son Bailey got an extra £15 "for the services of his Negro, Nell"); Daniel Sr.'s 1806 deed of gift to son William gives "one Negro woman named Sarah also one girl named Edy, likewise one girl named Hannah." (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
7. LORE, UNBACKED — the Huguenot origin tale (the line behind the couple): immigrant Jean De La Chaumatte of Rochechouart, France (son of attorney Daniel De La Chaumatte, grandson of Joseph Fourgeaud); family banned from the village ~1650, permitted to return by 1660; Jean fled ~1686, reached London 1692, married Elizabeth Bourgeois Bouvet at Threadneedle Street French Protestant Church 29 Sept 1695, sojourned in Martinique, arrived Stafford Co. VA ~1722, bought a farm on Elk Run. (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
8. Correction to earlier notes: Rich Creek was incorporated 17 April 1946 (Circuit Court of Giles Co; charter 1947), not 1926. (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
9. LORE, UNBACKED — possible twin pairs from year-collisions across compilations: Polly/Margaret (both 1790), Nancy/Rhoda (both 1797), Rachel/Malinda (both 1799); no exact same-day dates found — do not assert. (2026-10-02-lore-extract-batch3-shumate-hunt.md)
10. The "Archibald and Rhoda Shumate Burdette Bible" (published 1832, Cooperstown NY, held by descendant Mrs. Gertrude Burdett Bateson, Point Pleasant WV) supports RHODA as Archibald's wife — contradicting WikiTree's "Nancy Shumate Burdette (1788–1860)"; the Bible's full text is unobtained (top unobtained lead for the children's birth dates). (2026-10-02-dig-shumate-expanded.md)

### FLAGGED (not proposed)
- Tollison's dates CONTRADICTION: the stories give b. 27 Feb 1775 / d. 16 July 1861 (Fauquier Shumate compilation) vs the filed verification's b. 10 May 1781 Montgomery VA / d. 13 Jun 1867 — plus GenealogieOnline's d. 6 May 1861 Rich Creek (and its impossible 18 May 1863 Confederate enlistment for a man dead in 1861 — conflated) — adjudication needed; the research's more specific dates should adjudicate per the audit. (2026-10-02-audit-daniel-shumate.md; 2026-10-02-dig-shumate-expanded.md)
- Millicent's attached parents Isaac Callison × Elizabeth Callison: the Isaacs found in the wild (Isaac C. Callison b. ~1766–1772, Rupert Greenbrier Co; Isaac Callison 1766–1835) are too young to be her father — either a different, older Isaac or the attachment is wrong; the Callison cluster sits in Augusta/Pocahontas/Greenbrier, the same corridor. - Duplicate Daniel Shumate MPs (1749–1826): "Daniel Shumate III" (parents Daniel × Mary Shumate) vs "Daniel Shumate of Amherst County" (parents Daniel × Elizabeth Shumate), both husbands of Millicent. (2026-10-02-p293-verification.md)

Elisha b. 1761 who enlisted in the Rev War in Fairfax 1777) — a DIFFERENT man, a generation too old for the Montgomery line; do-not-merge. The story's published-history accounting says no histories name James or Sarah; the 3 vols. may hold Montgomery/Pulaski/Raleigh Wardens — digital version viewable on FamilySearch but NOT yet opened. McCreery's (McCleary's) Company, Augusta County VA militia, sworn 20 October 1778** — McAllister, *Virginia Militia in the Revolutionary War* §253 (verified verbatim; the full text sits in the folder's documents/). WeRelate's Black sketch independently states the same rank and company. Augusta militia 1778–1782 = frontier defense against Cherokee/Shawnee raids; company-level actions still unpulled. The story's "no service record" / "asserts no service for the parents" is now wrong for Samuel — the open-work item "the militia's rolls" is half-closed. VA; d. 1814 Augusta Co. VA), m. ~1749/1754 Augusta/Albemarle Co. VA; son John Black (b. 12/21 Dec 1755; d. 14/15 Jul 1849) m. (1) Jane Alexander 12 Aug 1777. Sources: WeRelate (citing wkfisher1's Black genealogy "A11 SAMUEL BLACK," Chalkley III:164, Kegley's Early Adventurers, Montgomery Co. Deed Bk A:39, 1801 probate receipt), Find a Grave 11620561, McAllister. (2026-10-02-dig-black-family-identity.md)
- **DAR pull result (story gap — the story notes the file's flags but not its substance):** DAR Ancestor #A010649 (John Black) recognizes **civil service ONLY — "Overseer of Road, Montgomery Co., 1781"** (service source: Summers, *Annals of SW Virginia*, Part 1, p. 749). The tree's "Lieutenant" for John is NOT supported by DAR. The story already flags the lieutenancy for testing — this is the test's negative result. (folder: andrew-aiken-alexander-catherine-stewart-thompson-alexander/2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **John's second wife:** Mary Breedon/Breeden, m. 1807 (b. 1765; d. 1826) — on the DAR record; not named in the tree/story material. (2026-10-02-dig-black-family-identity.md)
- **John's full sibling set — the Warden story's aunt/uncle generation:** from Samuel's 15 Sep 1801 legatee receipt to William Black, executor (Chalkley): John Black; Jane (m. Matthew Alexander); Margaret (m. Samuel Price); Mary (m. William Black); Martha; Nancy; Samuel; James; William. (2026-10-02-dig-black-family-identity.md)
- **Blacksburg whole-place lore (published family-history grade, not family-told):** William Black (John's brother, b. 14 Feb 1767) petitioned the VA General Assembly to establish a town on 38¾ acres of his land; incorporated 1798 and **named Blacksburg**; trustees: John Black, William Black, George Rutledge, James P. Preston, Edward Rutledge, John Preston, John Henderson (Montgomery Co. Deed Bk C:163); William gave land for a school and a cemetery, was a VA circuit-court judge 14 years, freed his slaves when the family moved to Clark Co. OH in 1814; John's Draper Road land became the majority of Virginia Tech's central campus. The story states "the lore is none" — this place lore belongs to the family. (2026-10-02-dig-black-family-identity.md)

## 3. Andrew Aiken Alexander × Catherine Stewart Thompson Alexander (P318) — "The Alexanders Above" (ch8 ~lines 11336–11795)

**Story state:** long honest telling; will book and deed books listed as "unexamined"; published Alexander histories as "the promise... unexamined"; lore stated as "none."

### FACTS TO ADD
- **The will is no longer unexamined — Chalkley verified verbatim (strongest add):** Lyman Chalkley, *Chronicles of the Scotch-Irish Settlement in Virginia* Vol. III — Andrew Alexander's will (Augusta Co. Will Book 7, p. 110), dated 28 June 1788, proved 20 Jan 1789: names wife **Catherine**, oldest son James, grandsons Andrew Hunter and John Alexander (infant, son of son Andrew — tract at the foot of South Mountain "at the place called the Wallowing Hole"), granddaughters (daughters of son Andrew), Samuel Hunter; executor: son-in-law **Samuel Hunter** (m. a daughter of the couple). Witnesses: Wm. Thompson, Samuel Wilson, Peter Frenger. **1762 joint deed** (Augusta Co. Deed Book 10, p. 197): Andrew Alexander and Catherine to Wm. Brown, 18 May 1762 — £50.5, 158 acres in **Beverley Manor**, adjoining Robert Wilson's land, Matthew Thompson's land, Thomas Brown's line. Both verified verbatim from the 1912 full text (IA). Pins the couple to Augusta Co. deeds 1762; gives Andrew's death window (will 28 June 1788 / proved 20 Jan 1789). (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
- **DAR pull for the son-in-law John Michael Black (story gap — supersedes the tree's "Lieutenant" framing):** DAR Ancestor #A010649, BLACK, JOHN — **civil service only: "Overseer of Road, Montgomery Co., 1781"** (Summers' Annals of Southwest Virginia, Part 1, p. 749). No military rank, no unit, no battles at DAR. DAR spouses: (1) Jane Alexander; **(2) Mary Breeden** — a second wife the story does not name. DAR note (2022): the patriot's daughter who married James Ross was named **Jane Black, not Mary Jane Black**. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)
- **Civil War service missing from the grandchildren section (J40 gap):** Hughes J. Warden (b. 13 Dec 1831) — "killed in the Civil War, it is believed in the Battle of Gettysburg" (1950 Beckley Post-Herald); **David J. Warden (b. 30 Nov 1838) — "also saw Civil War service"** (same article). Units UNIDENTIFIED (leads: NPS Soldiers & Sailors, WV Adjutant General's report). The story's grandchildren section names Hughes J. but omits both men's service entirely. (2026-10-02-dig-military-deepdive.md)

- **"Lonesome" nickname — origin account 1 now SUPPORTED (named source):** Lara M. Blake (Fayette County historian and school teacher) — Dave continuously whistled "some lonesome melody"; the name stuck so well **he placed his money in the bank under the name "Lonesome Dave."** The story carries only "the origin undocumented" — this supported account is the add. (2026-10-02-dig-jeremiah-williams.md)
- **Betsy McGriff parentage (NEW, 75%+, pending the marriage record):** Elizabeth "Betsy" McGriff — b. ~1781 at **Sinking Creek**, Montgomery Co.; m. David **16 Nov 1801, Knox County, Tennessee** (marriage record names her "Betsy"); parents **Thomas McGriff × Susan (Atkins) McGriff** (Thomas a son of **John McGriff**); d. **4 Oct 1858, Raleigh County, age 77, dropsy**. (2026-10-02-dig-jeremiah-williams.md)
- **Mary Garst born-at-sea lore — UNBACKED family tale (labeled):** DePauk compilation: "She was the daughter of German immigrants and was born during their voyage to America... her parents made a cradle for her from the **oven of the ship's stove**. No documentation regarding the truth of the story, or who Mary's parents were, has been discovered." Giles County Historical Society, *Giles County, Virginia, history – families* (c1982): "born about 1750 on a vessel sailing to America, **according to tradition**." Two independent family-history sources, both explicitly label it tradition/undocumented; no parents, no ship. Below the 75% book threshold for fact — usable only as a clearly labeled family tale. (2026-10-02-garst-born-at-sea-lore.md)
- **Jeremiah vitals/lore (75%+ facts):** ~200 acres total via three purchases from Michael Drake along Doe Creek (Pembroke–Pearisburg toward Mountain Lake); **35-acre grant surveyed 6 Sep 1819, granted 1 Sep 1821 by Gov. Thomas M. Randolph** (verified, marysfolly transcription); always 0 slaves in census/tax; married Mary Garst **1767–1770 in Fauquier County** (compiled); d. **May 1824** (WikiTree header 25 May 1824). Lore-only (unproved, tale-labeled): **Welsh birth tradition**; called **"The Elder"**; told a census taker his father William was b. Wales and his mother Ann b. Ireland (family-report grade). (2026-10-02-dig-jeremiah-williams.md; 2026-10-02-colonial-jeremiah-williams-status.md)
- **Published book mentions (context for the chapter's accounting):** Okey R. Stover, *History of Upper Paint Creek* (USGenWeb Archives text, opened): "He was a son of Jeremiah Williams who lived in Giles County, Virginia" — SUBSTANTIVE-MENTION of Jeremiah (calls Dave's wife "Mary McGriff" — treat his details as his own account); *Giles County, Virginia, history – families* (c1982) carries the "Jeremiah Williams, the Elder" family article (UNCONFIRMED-SNIPPET — book not opened; marysfolly transcription precise on author/title/imprint); Shirley G. Ferguson, *The Williams Family of W. Va. & Virginia* (1987; FamilySearch catalog) — UNCONFIRMED-SNIPPET, catalog describes David (1775/1776–1860) son of Jeremiah. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

## 5. Thomas McGriff × Susannah McGriff (P320) — "The McGriffs" (ch8 ~lines 12264–12677)

**Story state:** draft-stage chapter (in the ch8 PDF now); carries the tree's dates (Betsy b. 1781 Sinking Creek / d. 4 Oct 1858 dropsy); parent link UNPROVED; rescue tale (Elizabeth McGriff rescuing David Williams) carried as unbacked lore; the 1821-marriage/1802-birth tension carried; no Sinking Creek 1774 account; no Irish/Atkins material.

### FACTS TO ADD
- **The 7 Aug 1774 Sinking Creek Indian attack — verified verbatim from the 1906 full text (strongest add):** David E. Johnston, *A History of Middle New River Settlements and Contiguous Territory* (1906), printed pp. 42–44 (IA identifier historyofmiddlen00john; index entry "McGriff, Thomas 42-44") — "The four small boys playing in the water near the edge of the river, were, viz. Theophilus Snidow, Jacob Snidow, **Thomas McGriff**, and John Lybrook... Three of the little boys, Theophilus Snidow, Thomas McGriff and Jacob Snidow were captured by the Indians and carried away... they reached Pipestem Knob, now in Summers County, and there camped for the night... **two of the boys, Jacob Snidow, and Thomas McGriff slipped away from the camp... they crawled into a hollow log**... the Indians... even stood on the log in which the boys were hidden... the boys came out of their hiding place, struck through the woods, and made their way to Culbertson's bottom on the New River, where they were afterwards found by some of the scouts." And: "**Mr. McGriff shot and mortally wounded one of the Indians**, whose remains were years afterwards found under a cliff of rocks not far away from the scene of the tragedy." The captive was a small boy in 1774 — consistent with the tree's Thomas. Anita L. Aliff Williams's *Some Descendants of David "Lonesome Dave" Williams* manuscript asserts captured-Thomas = Betsy's father — the manuscript's assertion, not Johnston's; flag that distinction. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
- **The 1900 Darke County biography — verified verbatim from the 1900 full text:** *A Biographical History of Darke County, Ohio* (Lewis Pub. Co., Chicago, 1900), p. 324 (IA identifier biographicalhist00chi) — Price McGriff biography (Price b. 18 Aug 1820 Preble Co.; son of Patrick McGriff b. VA 1786; grandson of Thomas McGriff): "The grandfather, **Thomas McGriff, was a native of Ireland, and was of Scotch-Irish lineage.** He and his brother, together with two comrades, were held prisoners by the Indians for some time in the colonial epoch of our country's history, but one night while the guards were asleep all succeeded in escaping with the exception of one... On leaving the Old Dominion the father removed to Tennessee, and afterward to Ohio, coming to this state when the town of Cincinnati was entirely unimproved save by a little blacksmith shop. Mr. McGriff located on rented land between the big and little Miami rivers, and in 1811 began the development of a farm in the midst of the forest in Preble county. **He was married in Virginia to a Miss Atkins**, and they had a large family of **eleven children, five sons and six daughters**... The grandfather **died about 1828**, leaving a claim consisting of a quarter-section of land. He was a survivor of his wife for several years, **her death having occurred about 1831. She was a woman of resolute and noble nature, well-fitted to brave the hardships of pioneer life.**" That final sentence is the only published character portrait of the tree's Susannah. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)
- **The Ohio branch as identity corroboration (lead):** the 1900 bio's Thomas (captured boy → Virginia → Tennessee → Preble Co. OH 1811, d. ~1828) matches Johnston's captured small boy of Sinking Creek 1774 — two independent published accounts of a captured-and-escaped Thomas McGriff. Note the escape accounts conflict on details (1900: brother + two comrades, one failed to escape; 1914: "he and two brothers" all escaping) — not to be narrated as one clean event. (book-mentions-2026-10-02.md)

### NOT FOR THE STORY
- Susannah's maiden name: the 1900 bio gives only **"a Miss Atkins"** — the tree's "Susannah McGriff" (married-name label) is NOT confirmed by the passage; WikiTree's Susanna Adkins (dau. of Parker Adkins) is UNSOURCED beyond this bio. Maiden name = unresolved flag.
- *History of Darke County, Ohio* (Hobart, 1914), p. 425 (J.A. McGriff bio: Thomas "born in the north of Ireland... took part in much of the Indian warfare... captured... escaped") — UNCONFIRMED-SNIPPET (not opened this pass; the "native of Ireland" detail is now grounded by Johnston instead). Lead only.
- James R. Miller, *History of Summers County, WV* (1908), Part 3 — quotes Johnston; names the McGriff household at Sinking Creek but not Thomas personally in the quoted portion. UNCONFIRMED-SNIPPET. Lead only.
- Parent link: UNPROVED (unchanged) — no primary record names Thomas/Susannah as Betsy's parents; no severance candidate (needs above-75% to propose).
- Thomas's military service: NONE-FOUND via free lanes (no Rev War roll, pension, bounty, or DAR/SAR listing). Prime next step is the Montgomery Co. tax lists 1782–1800 to establish even his household.
- The 1950 centennial reprint of John Williams Sr.'s funeral story (the rescue tale's source) — copy still not located.
- The rescue tale itself: already in the story, labeled unbacked — no change.

Timothy Horsfield Jr. 14 Oct 1766) — NO son William anywhere. Additional nail: the surveyor married Johanna Christina Zeidig in Philadelphia in 1722, the same year William Jr. is said to be born in England — a child born in England in 1722 cannot be the son of a couple married in Philadelphia in 1722. The story's "flagged mismatch against the records' blank" upgrades to CONTRADICTED. — (2026-10-02-surveyor-exclusion-triple-checked.md; 2026-10-02-dig-interim-kill-confirmed-leads.md)

Harness's company, Romney payroll), ~1786 "to the Fort on the Buckhannon River," ~1796/97 to Warth's Bottom, then Frozen Camp before March 1818 ("He made the first improvement on the Upper Mill Creek Bottoms above the mouth of Frozen Camp... John Dodderidge (1745–1791)" matches the PUBLISHED John Doddridge of Doddridge's Fort — built Doddridge's Fort (stockaded cabins) ~1773 near present Avella, Washington Co. PA (Pennsylvania Historical and Museum Commission marker, erected 1947, hmdb.org/m.asp?m=20851; also on Wikipedia's list of Washington Co. forts) and Doddridge Chapel — the first Methodist Episcopal church erected west of the Allegheny Mountains (West Virginia Pioneer Families). The story says "the proof is not in hand" and uses the rank as a flag only — this upgrades the service claim itself (specific company/campaigns still open).
